# Voynich manuscript: strategies log

One row per strategy: method and control, result, verdict. Earlier work is summarised in FINDINGS.md.

| ID | Method and control | Result | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| v8:V-1.1 | Positive/negative calibration of spectral seriation: Isidore Etymologiae (Latin) cut into pages with the exact Voynich page/line lengths (225 pages); plain, with a slowly drifting homophonic letter cipher (23 letters switch variant at random times, logistic width 0.08), and with tokens redealt across pages. Stats: split-half |rho| (even vs odd lines seriated separately) and |rho| of recovered order vs true order | plain: split 0.46, ~truth 0.34 (TSP 0.33); drift cipher: split 0.94, ~truth 0.98 (TSP 0.69); redeal: split 0.02 / 0.07 | Method calibrated: a drifting key is recovered as a 1-D order; redeal kills it |
| v8:V-1.2 | Same seriation on Voynich (ZL3b, P/C/R lines, pages >= 30 tokens, words with ? dropped); null = tokens redealt; TSP path gain vs redeal null; recovered order vs binding order | split 0.88 (redeal 0.01); TSP gain 0.058 (Latin plain 0.035, cipher 0.154); Fiedler~binding |rho| 0.66; Fiedler~Currier B 0.80 | A strong, reproducible 1-D axis exists (split-half 0.88 vs 0.01 null; Latin+drifting key 0.94, plain Latin 0.46). At whole-book level it is mainly the A/B axis (0.80) plus section; it matches binding only through A/B (0.66) |
| v8:V-1.3 | Drift inside homogeneous strata (language x illustration): split-half |rho| vs within-stratum token redeal (10 seeds); matched Latin plain and drift-cipher blocks of the same size and line template | Currier A (all) n=114 split 0.66 (null 0.07, z 7.3) ~binding 0.59; Currier B (all) n=82 split 0.82 (null 0.09, z 11.8) ~binding 0.29; A herbal n=95 split 0.19 (null 0.09, z 1.3) ~binding 0.33; B herbal n=32 split 0.71 (null 0.17, z 4.7) ~binding 0.43; B bio (Q M) n=20 split 0.77 (null 0.13, z 8.3) ~binding 0.09; B stars (illus S, lang B) n=23 split 0.91 (null 0.10, z 10.7) ~binding 0.06; pharma (illus P) n=16 split 0.16 (null 0.17, z -0.1) ~binding 0.38 || controls: Currier A (all): Latin plain 0.26 (z 2.5), cipher 0.76 (z 13.3, ~truth 0.95); Currier B (all): Latin plain 0.40 (z 6.2), cipher 0.08 (z -0.7, ~truth 0.84); A herbal: Latin plain 0.07 (z 0.2), cipher 0.84 (z 12.4, ~truth 0.95); B herbal: Latin plain 0.74 (z 3.0), cipher 0.31 (z 0.4, ~truth 0.61); B bio (Q M): Latin plain 0.51 (z 7.8), cipher 0.46 (z 0.6, ~truth 0.39); B stars (illus S, lang B): Latin plain 0.84 (z 8.5), cipher 0.57 (z 1.8, ~truth 0.61); pharma (illus P): Latin plain 0.19 (z -0.6), cipher 0.69 (z 7.4, ~truth 0.21) | Split verdict. (a) Currier A herbal (95 pages, all hand 1): NO recoverable clock (split 0.19, z 1.3); a drifting key of the control strength in a block of the same size is recovered at 0.84 split / 0.95 truth, so A herbal pages are exchangeable: no writing order is recoverable there. (b) B strata (bio, stars, B herbal) show strong 1-D axes (0.71-0.91), but plain Latin topic continuity gives the same values (0.51-0.84) at these small n, so these axes are not yet shown to be time rather than topic/hand. (c) Pharma flat. Whole-A axis (0.66) is section, not time |
| v8:V-1.4 | Quire contiguity along the whole-book seriation: mean |rank gap| of same-quire page pairs vs quire labels permuted (300x), all pages and within A / within B | all: 38.0 vs 75.4 (z -24.7); within A: 25.9 vs 39.8 (z -12.4); within B: 20.8 vs 31.4 (z -8.2) | Quires are very compact in vocabulary space, also inside each language (z -12, -8). Consistent with quire = writing batch, but also with quire = subject; does not by itself give a clock |
| v8:V-1.5 | Inspection of within-stratum Fiedler orders (who sits next to whom). Control: hand labels, binding order | B herbal axis runs hand 3 (f94-95) / hand 2 -> hand 5 (f41, f48, f57) at the far end; B stars (all hand 3) and bio (all hand 2): recto and verso of the same leaf come out within 2 ranks in 8/11 stars leaves (5 directly adjacent) and 6/10 bio leaves (3 adjacent); order vs binding |rho| 0.06-0.09 | Within-B axes partly = scribe (B herbal). The leaf (r+v) behaves as a unit; quantified with a proper null in cycle 3 (V-3.x). Binding order inside stars/bio carries no vocabulary sequence |
| v8:V-2.1 | Graded-position test. Page B-score = mean token LLR (B vs A unigram, leave-one-page-out), from even lines and from odd lines separately; Spearman of the two inside each language after demeaning by section x hand. Null: tokens redealt within language x section x hand (10x). Controls: Isidore pages, same templates and group sizes, (G) one key drifting through the whole book, (D) two fixed keys with an abrupt switch | Voynich within A rho 0.52 (null -0.04, z 6.3); within B 0.51 (null 0.03, z 4.6). Control G (drift): A 0.39, B 0.54. Control D (discrete): A 0.21, B 0.51 | Not discriminating. Pages do hold a stable individual position on the A-B scale inside each language (z 6.3, 4.6 vs redeal), but the two-fixed-keys control (no drift at all) gives 0.21/0.51 from topic leakage alone, and the drifting-key control 0.39/0.54. Voynich A (0.52) exceeds both; B does not. A stable within-A position is real but cannot be credited to time rather than subject |
| v8:V-2.2 | Gap test: share of pages whose whole-page B-score lies in the middle third between the A and B medians. Controls G and D as above | Voynich 0.036 (7 pages); G 0.122; D 0.000 | Mostly a jump. Only 7/196 pages (3.6%) sit in the middle third; a drifting key gives 12%, two fixed keys 0%. The 7 intermediates are not scattered: 4 A pharma pages (f88v, f89r1, f89r2, f102v2), hand-3 A page f58r, hand-5 B page f57r, one A herbal (f90v1). Group medians order as A herbal (-0.96) < A pharma (-0.50) < hand-3 A (+0.08) < hand-5 B herbal (+0.48) < hand-2/3 B herbal and stars (+0.72..0.82) < hand-2 bio (+1.53). So the A->B scale is a ladder of section x hand groups with few in-between pages, not a smooth page-by-page drift (the gradual-section ordering itself is Timm 2014; the calibration against keyed controls is new) |
| v8:V-2.3 | Oddities as stages: inside A and inside B (demeaned by section x hand), Spearman of page B-score computed WITHOUT the feature-carrying words vs the feature. Calibration: same logic on controls with feature = share of tokens carrying a variant letter (letter excluded from score) | m/g line-end quota (B-A +0.074): A rho +0.15 p=0.11, B rho +0.02 p=0.88; q-initial share (B-A +0.060): A rho +0.02 p=0.85, B rho +0.19 p=0.084; ed share (B-A +0.197): A rho +0.11 p=0.26, B rho -0.37 p=0.00073; -ol/-or share (B-A -0.134): A rho -0.14 p=0.12, B rho -0.11 p=0.35; mean line length (B-A +2.010): A rho +0.16 p=0.097, B rho -0.04 p=0.7 || control G (drift) median rho A 0.30 / B 0.09; control D (discrete) median rho A nan / B -0.04 | Oddities are NOT stages along the A-B scale. Within A, none of m/g quota, q share, ed share, -ol/-or share or line length follows residual B-ness (all p > 0.09; drifting-key control gives median rho 0.30). Within B the strongest effect has the WRONG sign: pages richer in ed have less B-like other vocabulary (rho -0.37, p = 0.0007), transcription-robust in IT2a (-0.34, p = 0.002) and unchanged when all e-words are removed from the score (-0.39). B-ness is at least two-dimensional inside B (an ed-load axis trading off against other B-markers), so A/B cannot be one drifting scalar |
| v8:V-2.4 | Transcription robustness of V-2.3 (tools/v8_cycle2b.py): IT2a; score excluding ed-words vs excluding every e-containing word | ZL within B -0.37 / -0.39; IT within B -0.34 / -0.34; within A +0.09..+0.13 (n.s.) in both | Transcription-robust (Wells/IM77-style caveat: ZL and IT read the same pages). The within-B trade-off is a property of the text, not of one transliteration |
| v8:V-3.1 | Physical writing unit. Residual page similarity (Hellinger, double-centred within language x hand x illustration) for pair classes inside a quire: LEAF (r+v of one leaf), SIDE (conjugate leaves, same face of the unfolded sheet), CROSS (conjugate, opposite faces), OPEN (facing pages in current binding), OTHER. Null: page identities permuted within quire x group (2000x). Controls: Isidore poured into the same layout in book order, and in sheet-face order | Voynich ZL: LEAF +0.037 (n=117, z +4.3, p=0), SIDE +0.038 (n=110, z +4.2, p=0), CROSS +0.029 (n=109, z +2.4, p=0.008), OPEN +0.009 (n=44, z +0.0, p=0.478), OTHER -0.001 (n=721, z -9.2, p=1), SIDE-CROSS +0.009 (n=0, z +1.1, p=0.133), LEAF-SIDE -0.001 (n=0, z -0.1, p=0.548) | A herbal: LEAF +0.061 (n=49, z +4.6, p=0), SIDE +0.049 (n=47, z +3.4, p=0), CROSS +0.042 (n=47, z +2.3, p=0.0115), OPEN +0.020 (n=23, z +0.5, p=0.309), OTHER +0.003 (n=357, z -6.8, p=1), SIDE-CROSS +0.007 (n=0, z +0.6, p=0.26), LEAF-SIDE +0.012 (n=0, z +0.9, p=0.173) | B: LEAF +0.039 (n=36, z +3.5, p=0), SIDE +0.031 (n=38, z +2.3, p=0.012), CROSS +0.035 (n=35, z +2.8, p=0.0035), OPEN -0.000 (n=18, z -0.1, p=0.543), OTHER -0.006 (n=343, z -6.3, p=1), SIDE-CROSS -0.004 (n=0, z -0.4, p=0.667), LEAF-SIDE +0.008 (n=0, z +0.9, p=0.186) | IT2a: LEAF +0.034 (n=117, z +3.3, p=0.001), SIDE +0.043 (n=110, z +5.0, p=0), CROSS +0.027 (n=109, z +1.7, p=0.04), OPEN +0.010 (n=44, z +0.1, p=0.485), OTHER -0.000 (n=721, z -8.8, p=1), SIDE-CROSS +0.016 (n=0, z +2.1, p=0.02), LEAF-SIDE -0.009 (n=0, z -1.3, p=0.895) || control book order: LEAF +0.064 (n=117, z +7.9, p=0), SIDE +0.012 (n=110, z -2.2, p=0.986), CROSS +0.009 (n=109, z -2.5, p=0.996), OPEN +0.078 (n=44, z +6.7, p=0), OTHER +0.010 (n=721, z -6.8, p=1), SIDE-CROSS +0.002 (n=0, z +0.2, p=0.44), LEAF-SIDE +0.052 (n=0, z +6.3, p=0) | control sheet-face order: LEAF +0.034 (n=117, z +2.1, p=0.0215), SIDE +0.062 (n=110, z +6.9, p=0), CROSS +0.038 (n=109, z +2.3, p=0.0155), OPEN +0.040 (n=44, z +2.1, p=0.025), OTHER +0.008 (n=721, z -10.8, p=1), SIDE-CROSS +0.024 (n=0, z +3.0, p=0.0025), LEAF-SIDE -0.027 (n=0, z -3.2, p=1) | The writing unit is the BIFOLIO, not the book page sequence. Conjugate leaves are as alike as the two sides of one leaf (ZL: SIDE z +4.2, CROSS +2.4, LEAF +4.3), while facing pages of the current binding are at chance (OPEN z 0.0). Book-order writing (control) gives the reverse: LEAF/OPEN high, conjugates below chance. Same in A herbal, in B, and in IT2a. Face of the sheet (SIDE minus CROSS) is not established: ZL z +1.1, IT z +2.1, A herbal +0.6, B -0.4. NOT NEW in substance: Layfield & Davis 2026 (LSA, singulion hypothesis) report elevated within-bifolio similarity and text written before binding; this is an independent, control-calibrated replication with hand/language/section removed |
| v8:V-3.2 | Jumps along the current binding order: boundary score = residual similarity of the 2 pages before vs the 2 after (whole book, double-centred). Boundary types: quire edge, hand change, language change, section change, bifolio change in a quire with nothing else changing, and leaf change inside one bifolio. Calibration: partial key change (3 letters get a variant) planted at a random point in Isidore pages (30 trials) | mean score / share in sharpest 10%%: quire n=19 +0.041 / 0.21; hand n=33 -0.013 / 0.64; lang n=31 -0.023 / 0.68; section n=27 +0.056 / 0.07; bifolio n=68 +0.075 / 0.25; same-leaf n=123 +0.089 / 0.02; clean bifolio change n=44 +0.123 vs same-bifolio leaf change n=14 +0.131 (MW p=0.35); sharpest homogeneous jumps: f48r|f48v -0.015, f41r|f41v -0.011, f44r|f44v -0.009, f54r|f54v -0.004, f49r|f49v -0.001, f30r|f30v +0.001, f93r|f93v +0.005, f42r|f42v +0.008 || planted key change: median rank quantile 0.000, in lowest 5% in 90% of trials | Jumps sit at hand and language changes (64-68% of them in the sharpest 10% of boundaries), not at quire edges (21%) or section changes (7%); same-leaf boundaries almost never (2%). A 3-letter key change is caught 90% of the time, so a key change of that size inside a run would have been seen. (The list of homogeneous jumps here is contaminated by windows that cross hands; superseded by V-3.4) |
| v8:V-3.3 | Blind conjugate identification (prediction test): recto+verso merged into leaves; within quire x language x hand x illustration, is each leaf's most similar leaf (double-centred Hellinger) its conjugate? Chance = sum of 1/(k-1). Controls: Isidore poured in book order and in sheet-face order | ZL: 28/66 hits vs 12.9 expected (z +4.7); IT2a: 28/66 vs 12.9 (z +4.7); control book order 10/66 vs 12.9 (z -0.9); control sheet-face 51/66 vs 12.9 (z +11.8) | Prediction passes: from vocabulary alone, a leaf's nearest leaf is its physical conjugate 28/66 times against 12.9 expected (z +4.7, same in IT2a; transcription-robust, not replicated). Book-order control is at chance (10/66), sheet-wise control far above (51/66). The Voynich sits between: bifolios carry a shared vocabulary state, but weaker than a strict sheet-by-sheet text flow |
| v8:V-3.4 | Jumps inside homogeneous runs: boundaries in binding order whose 2+2-page window lies in one quire x language x hand x illustration group; boundary score as V-3.2. Calibration: a 3-letter partial key change planted at one such position in Isidore pages with the same layout (30 trials) | 106 homogeneous boundaries; sharpest 5%: f5r|f5v +0.020 (same leaf), f5v|f6r +0.018 (bifolio change), f6v|f7r +0.009 (bifolio change), f23v|f24r +0.033 (bifolio change), f52v|f53r +0.037 (same bifolio), f53v|f54r +0.018 (bifolio change); mean score same-leaf +0.130 (n=52) vs leaf change +0.124 (n=54) || planted key change: median quantile 0.000, in lowest 5% in 90% of trials | No hidden key change inside homogeneous runs. The 6 sharpest homogeneous boundaries (out of 106) are small and 5/6 fall at bifolio changes (f5v|f6r, f6v|f7r, f23v|f24r, f53v|f54r) or inside central bifolios (f5r|f5v, f52v|f53r) - i.e. the steps between writing units, not breaks of a cipher. A planted 3-letter key change would rank in the lowest 5% in 90% of trials |

Grade C guess: line-marker tick. See loops/v6_final.txt |
| v6:V-1.1 | Positive controls: does the test find the true order? | Each control's own order is the clear maximum: word MI excess along the true order +0.385 bits (z 79-123) vs <= 0.13 for any other order (spiral and boustrophedon partly reuse row pairs); junction +0.007 along the true order, ~0 elsewhere | Method works: a hidden column, diagonal or spiral order is found at z > 50 with this much text |
| v6:V-1.2 | Voynich ZL, all orders, excess over the full null (z) / over the order-specific null (z) | row: junction +0.175 (309 / 280), word +0.156 (34 / 35), first-first +0.046 (36 / 39). col: junction +0.002 (2.3 / -0.6), word +0.008 (1.4 / -0.2), first-first +0.023 (25 / 1.4). diag_dr: +0.001 / +0.002 / +0.008 (order-specific z <= 2.8). diag_dl: +0.001 / -0.004 / +0.007 (4.1). spiral: +0.045 / +0.070 / +0.035 (all from its row stretches; order-specific z 4.6 / -2.4 / 0.2). boustrophedon: +0.055 / +0.096 / +0.038 (half rows reversed, every value below row) | Left-to-right rows beat every other reading order on every MI measure, by 20-100x over columns and diagonals. Column first-first vs the full null (z 25) vanishes against the line-permutation null (z 1.4): it is the line-initial glyph bias, not a column order |
| v6:V-1.3 | Same, IT2a | row junction +0.162, word +0.143; col +0.0005 / +0.005; diag <= +0.003; spiral and boustrophedon below row | Transcription-robust: same ranking |
| v6:V-1.4 | Repeat rates (same word, same 2-glyph prefix) by order | All orders +0.000 to +0.007 over full null; order-specific z <= 4.4 (diag_dl prefix 3.3 ZL / 4.4 IT). Controls: true order shows NEGATIVE repeat excess (language avoids neighbours alike) | Faint similarity between nearby words in any direction (the known 'nearby copying' effect). No order is singled out |
| v6:V-1.5 | Direction: boustrophedon and right-to-left | MI is symmetric, so reversing a row changes nothing for word MI; the asymmetric junction falls from 0.175 to 0.055 under boustrophedon (half the lines reversed) | Rows are read left to right; reversing alternate lines destroys the junction link |
| v6:V-2.1 | Glyph grid: MI between a glyph and its right / lower / lower-right / lower-left neighbour, excess over the nulls | Voynich ZL: right +0.0039 (z 7), down +0.0017 (z 2.6), down-right +0.0009 (z 2.0), down-left +0.0012 (z 2.2). IT same pattern (right +0.0051; vertical 0.0012-0.0017). Column-written control: down +1.147 bits (z 226), right -0.0075. Diagonal-written control: down-right +1.195 (z 265). Row-written prose: right +0.037, vertical 0.000 | A text written down columns or diagonals shows > 1 bit of vertical/diagonal glyph information; the Voynich shows 0.001-0.002 bits, ~700x less, and less than its own horizontal value. No glyph-level column or diagonal reading order exists |
| v6:V-2.2 | Word under word: the next-line word below a word's midpoint (and +-one mean word width, 5.3 units) vs the next word in the row; junction, word-class, first-first MI and repeat rates | Row: junction +0.188 (z 205), first-first +0.052 (z 63). Below: junction +0.0005 (z 0.6), first-first +0.005 (z 2.6), word MI negative; below-right / below-left similar (same-prefix rate +0.006, z 2.8-6.7). Column-written control: below first-first +0.035 (z 30) | Every word-level link is >= 30x stronger along the row than down the page. The faint vertical prefix sharing is the known 'nearby-word copying' (Timm 2014), not a reading order |
| v6:V-2.3 | Offset profile: same-word and same-prefix rate between a word and the next-line word at horizontal offset dx = -16..+16 units, ratio to line-permutation null | Voynich: same-word ratio 1.01-1.28 and same-prefix 1.01-1.14 everywhere, with NO peak at dx = 0 (0: 1.08 / 1.02; ZL and IT). Prose control: <= 1.05 | Words resemble the line above about 10% more than a random line of the paragraph, at every offset alike. The page has local vertical similarity but no column alignment, consistent with Timm 2014 (known). Grid reading gains nothing |

Contradicts the forum claim of repeated vertical strings (SherriMM 2025) |

| v6:V-4.6 | Does the column follow any Voynich transition rule? Voynich 0.09 (rank 24/28). Test-only: Indus strong 0.84, IM77 0.78, khipu 0.34, Voynich words 0.05 | NOT SIGNIFICANT. What the model learns is 'administrative tablet lines' (Linear A + Proto-Elamite nearest Linear B and proto-cuneiform), a genre, not decipherment status; Voynich falls on the deciphered side. |

Acrostic control: 25.7% (z 10.7) | NOT a counter or rotating key: the best fixed order explains 4 points over chance, against 100% for a real counter. Closing glyph: y 47% of paragraphs vs 36% of other line finals; m 10% vs 15% | **One notation-like trace (transcription-robust):** the glyph that closes a paragraph depends on what the paragraph contains, beyond section and Currier language, more than other line finals do. Dante syllables (positive): z 47-129 at all u; chant words u = 2: z 13.6 | No quantisation of line length into a unit in glyphs or words (upper bound: the test sees the isosyllabic Dante line with z > 40). Lines are filled to the page, not counted out |

Voynich ZL x88 (z 53), IT x23 (z 13). Verbose Latin x33 (z 27), Manzoni x27 (z 26). Trigram-regenerated Voynich x29 (z 21). Glyph-shuffled Voynich x4 (z 9). Learned Voynich orders: ZL q d ch p r k o y e l sh a t n i s; IT s r t a sh n y i k l o d q e p ch (no agreement) | The search finds a real scale when there is one. First seed: Voynich ZL scores above prose and the trigram control, IT does not; the two learn unrelated orders. Settled in V-3.3 |

Learned orders differ on every run (e.g. ZL 'S q p e t i y o ch d k n a l r s' vs 'd o q S r a k ch l n y t i e s p') while chant's search returned the staff exactly. (The halves-agreement rho in the json is not usable: chant also scores 0.01 there, since a rotated or reversed order is equivalent) | **Null for transposition.** Voynich scores as high as chant, but shuffling word order inside lines does not lower the score at all, so it comes from the stock of words (rigid glyph order inside words), not from phrases repeated at another pitch. No stable scale exists: every run finds a different 'best' order. Rigid word-internal order is enough to fool a scale search; chant shows what a real scale looks like (one order, recovered blind) |

In chant it carries ten times more. No 'mode final' at section level |
| v5:V-3.4 | Order-free transposition: pairs of adjacent-word bigram types (a b), (a' b') where a -> a' and b -> b' are the same single-glyph change x -> y; obs vs 20 within-line word shuffles | **Voynich ZL 719 vs 596 (x1.21, z +4.1); IT 723 vs 603 (x1.20, z +4.4)**; trigram Voynich x1.02 (z 0.4). Top Voynich swaps: k/t 113, a/o 98, d/e 86, l/r 83, ch/sh 59 (e.g. okedy qokeey ~ otedy qoteey; al dal ~ ol dol; Cor Caiin ~ Sor Saiin) | A small real excess of 'parallel' glyph changes across neighbouring words, transcription-robust. But chant notation does NOT show it (melodic neighbours avoid it), so it is no notation fingerprint; it reads as neighbour assimilation: two adjacent words tend to share their gallows / vowel-like glyph / ch-sh choice (k with k, t with t), the same neighbour similarity as V-2.2. Worth a separate look as a 'concord' between words, not as melody. No positive control exists in the test set, so the test cannot say 'music' |

'Small corpus' cannot be the shared property of all four. |
| x1:X-1.5 | Native-size regime, same 202 statistics, same exact enumeration over 20,475 labelings and LOCO classifier (null 100 random labelings) | 0 perfect, 0 near separators (null mean 0.2, P = 1.0); cluster ratio 0.900 vs null 0.997, P = 0.17; LOCO AUC 0.792 (null mean 0.49, 95th 0.885, P = 0.11). Best statistics: share of the commonest final sign AUC 0.948 (U 0.27-0.55, D 0.02-0.41; Kafka letters 0.41), order information gain h2 0.906. Held-out P(U): Proto-Elamite 0.95, Linear B words 0.82, Ur III syllables 0.78, Linear A 0.75, Ur III names 0.71, Indus 0.68, Linear B syll 0.67 ... Voynich 0.26 (rank 19/28). Positive control: {Latin EDH, Linear B, runes, Unicode words} 30 near separators | NO SIGNATURE when corpus size is allowed to vary either. The classifier again ranks deciphered administrative corpora (Linear B, Ur III) with Linear A / Proto-Elamite / Indus, and Voynich among deciphered prose. |
| x1:X-1.6 | Planted size control: cut each deciphered corpus to Linear A / Indus / Proto-Elamite size and recompute every statistic with |AUC-0.5|x2 >= 0.8 in either regime (native: top-final-sign share, order gain h2; matched: bz2 reversed ratio). A size artefact would push the subsampled deciphered corpora to the U side of the U/D midpoint | top-final-sign share: deciphered on the U side 3/24 at every size (4/24 at full size), median shift +0.004; order gain: 2-3/24 (2/24 at full size), shift -0.001 to -0.007; bz2 reversed ratio: 2-3/24 (3/24). The statistics that DO move with size (Zipf slope, bigram Zipf slope, compressed bits per token, lzma real/shuffled ratio; shift 0.5-0.7 SD of the deciphered spread from 60k to 8.3k tokens) have U-vs-D AUC 0.47-0.70 | Control passes: deciphered corpora cut to undeciphered sizes do not acquire the leading near-signature; and the size-sensitive statistics do not separate the four in the first place. The near-separators are not size artefacts, but they are also not a signature (none separates, none survives the family-wise null). |
| x1:X-1.7 | Alternative definitions, both regimes (8 runs): Voynich as words instead of glyphs; IM77 instead of Wells Indus; deciphered set restricted to the 17 sign-level corpora; khipu added as a fifth undeciphered system. Exact enumeration, nperm 0 | Near separators 0 in 7 of 8 runs, 1 in native sign-only (null mean 0.5, P = 0.16). Cluster P 0.10, 0.40, 0.27, 0.38 (matched), 0.035, 0.20, 0.17, 0.37 (native). The one P < 0.05 (native, Voynich words) comes with LOCO neighbours runes words 0.88, Cervantes words 0.87, Linear B words 0.74: it is word-level granularity, not decipherment status. Indus seq_strong and IM77 score like seq_raw (transcription-robust, Wells and IM77 are ~70% the same objects) | No definition produces a signature; 1 of 8 cluster P values below 0.05 is the chance rate. |
| x1:X-1.8 | Family-by-family scan (18 families: length, lexicon, Zipf, Heaps, entropy, positional, edge, line locality, repetition, novelty, burstiness, long-range, compression, page self-similarity, graph, text, bigram), matched regime, exact null per family | 0 near separators in every family; cluster P lowest for positional (0.033), repetition 0.093, text 0.10; compression 0.39, long-range 0.65, page self-similarity 0.86. Bonferroni over 18 families: positional P = 0.59 | No family of statistics (in particular no compression, long-range or page-similarity property, the usual 'it is not language' tests) puts the four together. |
| x1:X-1.9 | Leave-Voynich-out: the four triads of three undeciphered corpora, exact enumeration over C(27,3) = 2,925 labelings, LOCO with 60-labeling null for the best triad | Indus + Linear A + Proto-Elamite: 5 near separators (null mean 0.9, P = 0.049), cluster P = 0.08, LOCO AUC 0.986 (null 0.46, P = 0.033). The three triads containing Voynich: 0 / 0 / 1 near separators, cluster P 0.26-0.69. Separators of the good triad: texts of <= 3 signs (U 0.67-0.85 vs D median 0.05), mean length 2-3, initial-sign entropy as high as the overall entropy (pos_h_init 0.98-1.17), next sign more predictable than previous (ent_back_minus_fwd +0.09 to +0.34 vs D ~0), MI across the line break (0.015-0.31). Post hoc (chosen after seeing Voynich fall out): x4 triads gives P ~0.13-0.2 | A weak shared profile for Indus, Linear A and Proto-Elamite: short lines whose first sign is free and whose order is fixed forwards. Not significant after correction. Voynich does not share it. |
| x1:X-1.10 | Implication test (genre vs decipherment). If the triad profile were about being undeciphered, adding deciphered administrative corpora of the same genre to the group should dilute it; if it is genre, it should strengthen. Group of six = triad + Linear B syllables + proto-cuneiform + Ur III owner names (chosen a priori as the deciphered short administrative / label corpora); exact enumeration over C(28,6) = 376,740 labelings; LOCO with 60-labeling null. Planted size control on the triad separators (deciphered cut to 8.3k tokens) | Six-group: 9 near separators (null mean 0.0, P = 0.0005), cluster ratio 0.742 (P = 0.013), LOCO AUC 0.952 (null 0.48, 95th 0.72, P = 0.016). Triad LOCO already ranked Linear B syllables 0.75, proto-cuneiform 0.66, Ur III names 0.61, Linear B words 0.60 just below the three. Planted: cutting deciphered corpora to Linear A size leaves the triad separators on the deciphered side (shifts 0.000-0.02, e.g. texts <= 3 signs 7/24 on the U side both cut and uncut) | The shared profile belongs to the GENRE (short administrative and label lines), and deciphered corpora of that genre have it more cleanly than the undeciphered triad alone. It is not caused by corpus size. It is not an undeciphered signature. |
| x1:X-1.final | 202 statistics x 32 corpora, exact enumeration, LOCO + permutation, planted size control, 8 alternative definitions, 18 family scans, triad and genre tests (cycles 1-3) | The four: 0 perfect and 0 near separators in both regimes (null mean 0.2), cluster P 0.34 / 0.17, LOCO AUC 0.73 (P = 0.17) / 0.79 (P = 0.11); Voynich always lands among deciphered prose (P(U) 0.09 / 0.26). Corpus size cannot be the shared property (Voynich 155k glyphs is larger than 16 of 24 deciphered corpora) and subsampling deciphered corpora to undeciphered sizes does not give them the leading near-separators. Indus + Linear A + Proto-Elamite share a weak short-administrative-line profile (LOCO 0.986, P = 0.033 uncorrected, ~0.13 after the 4-triad correction), which strengthens when deciphered Linear B syllables, proto-cuneiform and Ur III names join (9 near separators, P = 0.0005; LOCO 0.95, P = 0.016) | NO UNDECIPHERED SIGNATURE, beyond corpus size or otherwise. Nothing in the sign statistics of these four systems is unlike deciphered writing as a class; three of them look like deciphered administrative writing of the same genre and Voynich looks like deciphered alphabetic prose. What keeps them undeciphered is outside the sign stream (no bilingual, no known language, few anchors), not a statistical anomaly. Not 'cracked'; nothing read. |

Planted key controls (body glyphs shifted by the key rank on the frequency order, all glyphs or word-initial only): z 52-160 | Nothing. Would kill the marker reading too: the rule vanishing on lines that are interrupted by drawings |
| v9:V-1.1 | POSITIVE CONTROL. Planted corpus from a known 3-ring volvelle (20 cells/ring, Zipf-like labels, kernel 0.5 on one step + 0.2 on another + 0.3 spread, reset each line, Voynich line lengths); full search run blind | Ring 0: hard volvelle gain +1.019 b/tok (oracle +1.027; Markov-1 +0.880, Markov-2 +0.964); ring 1 +0.900 (oracle +0.989; M1 +0.826); ring 2 +0.971 (oracle +1.008; M1 +0.839). True ring-0 kernel: step 14 = 0.52, step 2 = 0.22; recovered on n = 40: step 28 = 0.519, step 4 = 0.212 (the 20-ring embedded twice, steps doubled: exact). Word level: hard volvelle 7.216 b/tok, true volvelle 7.083, M1 7.561, word trigram 10.009 | PASS. The search recovers a planted volvelle almost to oracle likelihood, recovers its turning rule exactly (up to the ring embedding), and on volvelle text it beats Markov-1/2 at equal or smaller budget. Reset vs no-reset is also recovered (no-reset loses 0.25-0.35 b/tok per ring) |
| v9:V-1.2 | Voynich ZL3b (P lines), same pipeline | Per-ring sequential information is tiny: Markov-1 gains +0.043 / +0.032 / +0.019 b/tok (prefix/core/suffix ring). HARD volvelle: -0.036 / -0.052 / -0.134 (worse than iid). Soft (fuzzy-cell) circulant HMM: +0.062 / +0.065 / +0.036. Learned kernels are almost flat: entropy 5.03-5.32 bits (flat = 5.32-5.58); best single step carries 6-11%. Word level: hard volvelle 9.566 b/tok, M0 9.344, M1 9.250, word trigram 8.471, plain word-tuple unigram 8.272 | FAIL. No ring order with a simple turning rule predicts the next word's prefix, core or suffix better than drawing it at random; the turning kernel the search finds is close to uniform (i.e. "turn the disc anywhere"). Independent rings also lose ~1.1-1.3 b/tok against models that know which prefix goes with which suffix inside a word: Voynich words are not free combinations of ring sectors |
| v9:V-1.3 | NEGATIVE CONTROL. Isidore Latin under verbose substitution (v7 cipher), same pipeline | M1 gains +0.004 / +0.002 / +0.010; hard volvelle -0.060 / -0.041 / -0.050; soft +0.005 / +0.002 / +0.012; kernel entropy 4.94-5.21 bits. Word level hard 8.201, M1 8.034, trigram 6.780, tuple unigram 6.588 | Fails as expected (control behaves). The Voynich numbers have the same shape as this natural-language control |
| v9:V-1.4 | NEGATIVE CONTROL. Manzoni Italian under verbose substitution (v5 table) | M1 +0.030 / +0.030 / +0.036; hard volvelle -0.022 / -0.028 / -0.035; soft +0.027 / +0.028 / +0.034; kernel entropy 4.91-5.10. Italian: +0.013 / +0.008 / +0.003 (no-reset slightly better) | Small, right-signed Voynich preference for a per-line reset (0.01-0.04 b/tok), larger than the controls, matching the known line-start vocabulary; but it is a start-distribution effect, available to any model with a line-start state, not evidence for discs |
| v9:V-1.6 | Soft (fuzzy-cell) circulant HMM beats within-ring Markov-1 on Voynich (+0.062 vs +0.043 etc.) but not on the controls (Latin +0.005 vs +0.004, Italian +0.027 vs +0.030) | Excess over M1: Voynich +0.019 / +0.033 / +0.017; controls -0.003..+0.001 | A small Voynich-only advantage of a 40-48-state hidden chain with a near-flat kernel and 6-7% 'stay' (step 0) mass: a slowly persisting hidden state, i.e. line/page mode clumping (N1/N3, §9), not turning. Tested in cycle 2 with a within-line shuffle |

Latin -0.8, Italian +2.6, Spanish +2.7, planted graph -1.2, grid-60 -1.3 | The Voynich 'walk' forgets after one step: no lag-2 proximity at all. This is the signature of a first-order succession rule, not of motion through a space. Transcription-robust |

Letting the turning depend on the previous word does not rescue the volvelle: worse than iid on every Voynich ring, while a plain Markov chain with the same conditioning gains 0.02-0.06 b/tok. Most of what the previous suffix tells about the next word is the junction coupling (iid x suffix already gets +0.037 on the prefix ring), which a disc that turns by an amount cannot express |

The word trigram matches J_in (0.066) and WW (0.347) and clumps more (1.30). No model reproduces CLOSE or the full CLUMP: the line end and the per-line make-up need a line-level plan, which a disc turned word by word cannot supply |

| v13:V-13.6 | Real illustrated-herbal control: Gerard, The Herball (1636), archive.org item mobot31753000817756 (Missouri Botanical Garden copy), pages n100-499 via iiif.archive.org at !1000,1000 (scratch cache only), OCR from the item's djvu.xml, cleaned to alphabetic words of 3+ letters seen 10+ times. Same blind image features; same-leaf contrast (pages n, n+1); wrong-leaf shifts 1, 3, 10 | Drawing area~OCR log words: same leaf r=+0.39, shift 1 +0.43, shift 3 +0.03, shift 10 +0.03. Sanity: blind text-ink~OCR words r=-0.09; drawing area~blind text-ink r=-0.62 | CONTROL UNUSABLE. At <=1000 px, Gerard's dense printed text (~60 lines a page) merges into large components and is read as drawing, so 'drawing' here largely means 'text block', and the sign is the opposite of the layout trade-off one would expect. The segmenter is tuned to the Voynich's large hand and does not transfer. The pipeline's sensitivity therefore rests only on the planted controls (V-13.3, V-13.5) and the Voynich self-check (V-13.1, text-ink r 0.79). Would fix: full-resolution scans or a herbal with manuscript-sized script |

Page-level offset profile k0 -0.11 (k-1 +0.22): no spike | Robust to transcription (transcription-robust, not replicated), to segmentation, to paragraph position and to layout covariates. But it lives ONLY inside a leaf: across pages of the same section there is no coupling (k0 -0.11). That pattern suits something physical between the two sides of one sheet |
| v13:V-13.8 | Show-through test. Over all 160 leaf sides (114 herbal): this side's root-zone edges against its OWN benched-gallows rate (reverse side partialled out) and its REVERSE side's benched rate (own side partialled out), plus section/language/hand; null = text assigned to the wrong leaf (5000x) | All: own -0.19 (p 0.013), reverse +0.27 (p 0.0002); herbal: own -0.25 (p 0.006), reverse +0.33 (p 0.0002); reverse blind text-ink +0.03 / +0.14 | The stronger term is the REVERSE side. Pages whose back carries many heavy benched gallows show more 'edges' in the lower drawing zone, which is what ink show-through or bleed of the boldest glyphs would do. The within-leaf difference then turns this into a spurious negative own-side coupling. A weaker own-side term (-0.25) remains and is not separable here. DEMOTED to a probable imaging artifact. No verified text-follows-drawing link |
| v13:V-13.final | 18 blind drawing features x 21 text features, 175 pages (119 herbal); nulls: within-section shuffle (shown INVALID by derangement), offset profile, same-leaf recto/verso contrast with sign-flip and leaf-permutation nulls, wrong-leaf shifts, planted image-driven and pure-drift texts, IT2a, segmentation variants, show-through partial test; external herbal control attempted (Gerard) | Length/vocabulary: no coupling within leaves (drawing edges~log words r +0.005, drawing area~log words -0.13 ns), while planted length coupling of r >= 0.4-0.5 is caught 6/6. Cycle-1 'busier drawing, fewer ch/sh' (r -0.39) is equally strong with the NEXT page's drawing (-0.35): drift. The only FW survivor, root-zone edges ~ benched gallows (same leaf -0.43, herbal -0.52, IT2a same), is predicted more by the REVERSE side's benched rate (+0.33) than by the page's own (-0.25): probable show-through. Gerard control unusable (segmenter reads dense print as drawing) | NO: the text does not track its own drawing at detectable strength. Not in length, not in vocabulary richness, not in any of 12 glyph families, beyond section, language, hand and local drift. Upper bound: page-level couplings of r ~0.4+ for length are excluded, but glyph-rate couplings below r ~0.35 cannot be seen with 57-80 leaves. The detection of planted effects is calibrated only on planted Voynich-vocabulary texts (the real-herbal positive control failed for measurement reasons), so a weak descriptive relation is not ruled out. Methodological gain: drawing style and text style drift together over ~2 pages, so any image-text test that shuffles within section is invalid. Use the same-leaf contrast plus a check against the reverse side |
| v11:V-4.1 | Model-free one-way test: excess direction asymmetry | Planted route 0.444 / 0.452 bits; planted grid -0.018 / -0.017; Voynich ZL 0.070 / 0.064, IT 0.056 / 0.054; Latin 0.278 / 0.280; Italian 0.364 / 0.387 | Voynich word succession is only weakly one-way: 4-6x less than Latin or Italian, 7x less than a route. A one-way route is excluded. (Side observation: the Voynich word chain is unusually reversible for a language; whether the v6 junction rule explains the non-metric part is tested in cycle 5) |

Latin 0.42 / 0.29 / 0.36 / 0.55; Italian 0.34 / 0.36 / 0.37 / 0.83 | No 2-D symmetric geometry of any signature (attractive, antipodal, mixed) reaches the asymmetric bilinear model on Voynich; antipodal is the worst. Voynich is not a walk across a wheel either |

Latin 0.02 / 0.04 (5%), Italian 0.14 / 0.15 (18%), planted grid 0.01, line-shuffle 0.00 | A 150-cell table of (end glyph, start glyph) explains most of what any 2-D word model finds in Voynich succession; in natural language it explains little. This is the v6 junction effect seen from the word side: the 'hidden space' is a glyph-junction rule, not a geography. Transcription-robust |
| v11:V-5.2 | What does a map add on top of the junction? juncdist2 minus junc | Voynich ZL +0.11 / +0.09, IT +0.10 / +0.08; line-shuffle (topic only) dist2 0.05; Latin +0.56 / +0.46; Italian +0.38 / +0.37; planted grid +0.77 / +0.83 | After the junction, a 2-D map adds about 0.1 bit in Voynich, half of which a line-shuffled text also gets (topic/section). Word-level 'location' carries at most ~0.05 bits per step beyond junction and topic, 5-8x less than in Latin or Italian |
| r1:R-1.1 | (a) N=10,000 random partitions per script on the real corpus, N=5,000 on each of 3 global shuffles (tokens permuted across corpus, line/word/numeral skeleton kept) and 3 planted corpora; (g) 60 random restarts per run climbed on split A only (2-fold inside A, k=2..6), then the same B/C tests; 2 shuffles + 2 planted per script | (a) Real: Voynich 2,611 stage-1 / 198 of 200 replicate; LA 47 / 13 of 47; PE 76 / 19 of 76.
