| v26:V-26.4.3 | KILL TEST for the margin: the planted genome (copy + ch/sh + drift + m/g + sec/pos) applied to the Voynich and to Latin, where it is plainly wrong | V: AUC 1.000 / 0.997, negative 0.94 / 0.92, margin 0.06 / 0.08. LA: 1.000 / 0.996, negative 0.98 / 0.96, margin 0.02 / 0.04 | The margin is KILLED as a stand-alone metric: a wrong genome that compounds its own effects gets a margin as small as the Voynich winner's while being caught at AUC 1.0. Only the absolute AUC (and the floor of an idempotent re-forge, 0.52-0.60) can be trusted |
| v26:V-26.4.4 | Verdict on the requested contrast, absolute AUC with fresh seeds | Voynich best 0.796 / 0.843 (floor of idempotent base re-forge 0.52; v21 floor 0.54-0.68). Latin best 0.842 / 0.855 (floor 0.52-0.60). Planted text, true genome 0.956 | The Voynich does NOT reach the floor, and the herbal does NOT stall clearly above the Voynich (difference 0.01-0.05, within seed spread 0.72-0.85). The planted control shows the scribe family cannot even re-generate its own output (0.956), so 'generable' cannot be certified with refit-then-regenerate generators. Grade A for the negative statement (no genome reached the floor); the Voynich-vs-herbal contrast is NOT shown |
| v26:V-26.4.5 | REPLICATION: independent Voynich restart 1 (different random 64, 10 generations, 344 evaluations) | Random phase already best with drift (junc=2 + pfl + b_dr 6.88: 0.884); detours through sec + width + m/g quota + drift (0.86); final winner junc=2 + width + chain 0.23 + drift 7.19 (running mean 0.846-0.871) | The same four mechanisms as restart 0 (rich key, width, chain, drift), reached independently. Grade B: paragraph drift of q-/a-/l- word make-up from the line above is the single mechanism the v21 ladder lacked |

| v30:V-30.1 | DIALECT LADDER: cheapest context-sensitive rewrite (<= 30 ordered rules, one-pass transducer) turning Currier A into B, found by stochastic greedy search and scored on held-out pages (JSD of char 2/3-grams + words). Ladder from real 1350-1450 pairs run through the same pipeline: Commedia copies by different scribes (CATMuS), Bavarian vs Alemannic and Ripuarian (ReF), Latin vs Italian (CATMuS), Czech edition vs pre-Hus digraph spelling, German/Latin/Czech/Italian cross pairs, 3-letter-swap and full-substitution keys. Nulls: page-split A/A, B/B, ZL/IT, Markov resyntheses | Raw excess: nulls 0.00-0.04; B hand 2/3 0.02; Commedia 0.26; Bav->Alem 0.45; A->B 0.46 (herbal 0.48); same dialect other texts 0.58; Latin->Italian 0.78; reform 0.94; 3-swap key 1.23; unrelated 1.3-1.6; substitution 1.94. Nulls never gain on held-out data | A->B sits on the DIALECT rung, far below language or cipher-key change. Kills (A/A ~ A/B; A->B > Latin->Italian) not fired. Grade B |
| v30:V-30.2 | Planted 4-rule rewrite of A (positive); out-of-sample transfer (herbal-learned rules applied to unseen A pharma vs unseen B bio/stars/hand 3) against 300 random and 150 shuffled-rhs rule sets, calibrated with German and Latin transfers; 22,000 random 6-rule sets (share improving fit, survivors re-tested) | Plant closed 0.44-0.83, 1-2 of 4 rules exact. Transfer: -8% / -4% / -5% distance (German dialect -10%, to another dialect +1.5%, reform -53%); all random sets and 146/150 shuffled sets worse. Random sets improve A->B 1% (dialect 1%, same-language 0-1%, language/reform/cipher/unrelated 13-54%) | A->B rewrite is real and section-independent, and dialect-sized on a second, search-free ruler. Grade B |
| v30:V-30.3 | Guided search (60% proposals from over/under-represented substrings), 2 seeds; reversed directions; edge-only vs context-free rule families | Closure@30 A->B 0.47/0.48, herbal 0.60/0.67, B->A 0.45 vs dialect 0.27-0.32, Commedia 0.17-0.25, Latin->Italian 0.28-0.40, reform 0.56-0.69. Top rules 4/4 runs: A -chy/-chol/-chor/-or -> B -chedy/-chdy/-edy; word-initial cth reduced. Edge/free 0.38/0.38 (reform 0.41/0.86, cipher 0.37/0.75, dialect 0.20/0.18). Direction ratio 1.07 (dialect 1.08, reform 1.19) | A->B = dialect-sized but reform-regular, ending-centred, symmetric shift; not a letter key. Grade B for the ending family, C for symmetry. Not cracked (nothing outside the manuscript predicted) |
| v30:V-30.1.0 | NULLS: A split vs A split, B vs B, Latin vs Latin, Bavarian vs Bavarian (page halves of one corpus); A vs a trigram Markov resynthesis of A, same for B; ZL vs IT transliteration (disjoint A pages) | ex0: A/A +0.005, B/B -0.007, A herbal/A herbal +0.010, ZL/IT -0.009, Markov A +0.039, Markov B +0.031; held closure always <= 0 (rewrites learned on fit make held worse, by 0.003-0.02) | Nulls pass: same-source pairs sit on the floor and the search cannot buy held-out gain there. The rewrite gains below are not search artefacts |
| v30:V-30.1.1 | Within-B scribes: B hand 2 (mostly biological) -> B hand 3 (stars, herbal), 2 seeds | ex0 0.023 (A/A null 0.005, Markov 0.03-0.04); closure -0.06 | Hands 2 and 3 of Currier B write statistically the same text: distance at null level, across different sections. B is one homogeneous system |
| v30:V-30.1.2 | LADDER of raw distance ex0 (mean of 2 seeds, 4,500 words per side) | same work other scribes (Commedia) 0.26; dialect Bavarian->Alemannic 0.45; **Voynich A->B 0.46 (B->A 0.49; herbal A->herbal B 0.48 at small budget, where Bav->Alem is 0.42 and Commedia 0.28)**; same dialect other texts 0.58; far dialect Bav->Ripuarian 0.71; Latin->Italian 0.78; spelling reform 0.94; 3-letter-swap cipher 1.23; unrelated languages 1.29-1.62; full substitution 1.94 | A->B falls on the DIALECT rung: above two scribes copying the same work, level with Bavarian vs Alemannic, below everything language-level and far below any cipher key change (even swapping 3 letters costs 2.7x the A/B distance) |
| v30:V-30.1.3 | Shape of the cheapest rewrite: held closure after 10 and 30 rules, share of held gain carried by the top 3 rules (top3) | closure@30: A->B 0.48 (seeds 0.60 / 0.37), B->A 0.40, herbal 0.32; dialect 0.26; Commedia 0.13-0.16; Latin->Italian 0.35; reform 0.52; unrelated 0.37-0.47; ciphers 0.44 (search does not finish a 25-letter key in 30 rules). top3: A->B 0.56 (seed 0: 0.76), reform 0.40, dialects 0.29-0.35, languages 0.23-0.30 | A->B is the most CONCENTRATED rewrite on the ladder. Seed 0: two rules, chy -> edy at word end (+0.060) and or -> edy anywhere (+0.110), give 0.17 of the 0.23 held gain. The cheapest A->B move is to turn A's -y / -or endings into B's -edy. On herbal-only text the concentration falls to 0.38 (dialect-like) |
| v30:V-30.1.4 | Kill tests for the brief: (i) split A vs split A costs as much as A vs B? (ii) A->B costs more than Latin->Italian? | (i) 0.005 vs 0.46: no. (ii) 0.46 vs 0.78: no | Neither kill fires. A/B is a real but sub-language distance |
| v30:V-30.2.1 | POSITIVE planted control on the Voynich alphabet: A page-half X vs other A page-half Y rewritten by 4 planted rules (o->a word-initial, dy->dey word-final, k->t, aiin->ain); cycle-1 search, 15 rules, 2 seeds | Planted excess 0.53-0.57 (larger than A->B 0.46). Held closure 0.64 / 0.44; exact planted rules recovered 1/4 and 0/4 (k->t never proposed: the random proposal hits one given single-glyph rule ~1% per step) | The cycle-1 search measures DISTANCE and closure reliably (nulls flat, plant closed by half) but is too weak to recover exact rules. Rule lists in cycle 1 are therefore frequency trades, not a reconstructed rulebook; the 'chy/or -> edy' reading stays grade C |
| v30:V-30.2.2 | OUT-OF-SAMPLE TRANSFER: 20 rules learned on ALL herbal A -> ALL herbal B, applied unchanged to A pharma (never seen) and scored against B biological, B stars, B hand 3 (never seen). Controls: 100 random rule sets of the same size from the same proposal pool; 50 sets with the learned lhs and shuffled rhs. Calibration: Bav1->Alemannic rules applied to other Bavarian texts (Bav2) vs Alemannic and vs Ripuarian; Latin->Italian prose rules applied to Latin vs the Commedia; Czech reform rules applied to the other half of Dalimil | Voynich pharma->bio 1.000 -> 0.920 (-8%), ->stars 0.813 -> 0.781 (-4%), ->hand 3 0.826 -> 0.782 (-5%); random sets median +0.41..+0.48, best random always worse than d0; shuffled rhs -0.5%..+4%. German Bav2->Alem -10%, Bav2->Ripuarian +1.5% (rules do not carry to another dialect); Latin->Commedia -13%; Czech reform -53% | PASSES: the A->B rewrite learned on herbal pages predicts B's statistics in sections it never saw, beating all 300 random rule sets and 146 of 150 shuffled-rhs sets, with the same size of effect as a real dialect rewrite (-4..-8% vs -10%) and far below a spelling reform (-53%). The learned herbal rules again build B's -edy/-ed endings (eor->dy, cho->edy word-final, ees->edy, hai->hed, har->hdy word-final). Grade B: the A/B difference is one systematic, section-independent shift of dialect size |
| v30:V-30.2.3 | MASSIVE RANDOM GUESSING: 2,000 random 6-rule sets per pair (22,000 sets); share that improves the fit distance; top 1% by fit re-scored on held-out pages (survivors) vs 40 random sets | Share improving fit: A->B 0.01; A/A, B/B, B2/B3 null 0.00; Commedia 0.00; Bav->Alem 0.01; same dialect other texts 0.01; Latin->Italian 0.13; reform 0.17; 3-swap cipher 0.28; German->Italian 0.54. Survivors' held gain: A->B +0.008 (70% positive), Bav->Alem -0.001 (50%), Bav/Bav +0.003 (70%), Commedia -0.003 (20%), nulls -0.003..-0.007 (5%), Latin->Italian +0.031, reform +0.072, cipher +0.063, unrelated +0.072 (100%) | A second, search-free ruler gives the same rung: random rewrites almost never help A->B, exactly as for a dialect or same-language pair, while they often help between languages, across a spelling reform or a key change. Grade B |
| v30:V-30.3.0 | POSITIVE: planted 4-rule rewrite of A (as V-30.2.1), guided search, 15 rules, 2 seeds | Held closure 0.83 / 0.48 (unguided 0.64 / 0.44); exact rules recovered 2/4 and 1/4, plus near-equivalents (dy->dey without context; k->'' and dy->'' partial) | Guided search recovers most of a planted Voynich-alphabet rewrite; good enough to compare rule SHAPES, still not a guaranteed rulebook |
| v30:V-30.3.1 | Guided ladder, 2 seeds, 30 rules, same splits as cycle 1; nulls rerun | Nulls: A/A, B/B, B2/B3 held closure <= 0.10, excess +-0.02. closure@30 (seed 0 / seed 1): **A->B 0.47 / 0.48; herbal A->herbal B 0.60 / 0.67; B->A 0.47 / 0.43**; dialect Bav->Alem 0.30 / 0.29 (small budget 0.32 / 0.29; reversed 0.27); same dialect other texts 0.35; Commedia 0.25 / 0.17 (reversed 0.20); Latin->Italian 0.40 / 0.39 (reversed 0.28); spelling reform 0.69 / 0.64 (reversed 0.56); 3-swap cipher 0.67; full substitution 0.56; German->Italian 0.51 | Seeds now agree. At the SAME raw distance as a dialect pair (ex0 0.46-0.48 vs 0.42-0.49), the A->B gap closes 1.6-2.2x as far as any dialect or same-language pair, close to a spelling reform. A->B = dialect-sized distance with reform-like regularity |
| v30:V-30.3.2 | Which rules carry the gain (held-out marginal gain, guided runs, 4 runs) | Every run's top rules build B's -dy / -edy endings out of A's -y/-or/-ol endings: A->B chy->edy (+0.060, +0.047), eol->edy (+0.040), or->edy word-final (+0.058), o->dy word-final (+0.028); herbal hol->hdy word-final (+0.061), chy->edy word-final (+0.039), hor->hdy (+0.051), hor->edy word-final (+0.033); and word-initial cth deleted or turned into ch/lch (+0.016..+0.039 in 4/4) | Grade B (reproduced in 4/4 guided runs, 2 unguided runs, and the cycle-2 transfer rules): the cheapest A->B rewrite is (1) A's -chy/-chor/-chol endings -> B's -chedy/-chdy, (2) fewer word-initial cth. Two clusters carry about half the gain; the rest is scattered frequency trading |
| v30:V-30.3.3 | Direction asymmetry X->Y vs Y->X (closure@30, guided, mean of 2 seeds) | A->B 0.48 vs B->A 0.45 (ratio 1.07); spelling reform 0.67 vs reversed 0.56 (1.19, many-to-one: diacritics are lost); dialect 0.30 vs 0.27 (1.08); Latin->Italian 0.40 vs 0.28 (1.40); Commedia 0.21 vs 0.20 | A/B is nearly symmetric, like a dialect pair; no sign that one side was derived from the other by a lossy many-to-one reform. Grade C (one pair per rung; would kill: an asymmetry > 1.2 on IT2a) |
| v30:V-30.3.4 | WHERE the rewrite lives: same guided search restricted to word-edge rules only vs context-free rules only (20 rules, seed 0) | edge / free closure: A->B 0.38 / 0.38; B->A 0.30 / 0.39; spelling reform 0.41 / 0.86; 3-swap cipher 0.37 / 0.75; dialect 0.20 / 0.18; Latin->Italian 0.25 / 0.35; Commedia 0.17 / 0.08; same dialect 0.30 / 0.28 | Letter-level systems (reform, key change) are closed by context-free rules at twice the edge rate; scribal habits (Commedia) live at word edges; dialects are even. A->B is EVEN, like a dialect, but at twice a dialect's level: the difference sits in glyph clusters (chy/chol/chor vs chedy), at the end of words and inside them, not in a letter-for-letter key. Grade B |
| v30:V-30.F1 | Where does the A->B distance fall? | Raw excess (mean of 2 seeds): nulls 0.00-0.04; B hand 2 vs B hand 3 0.02; same work other scribes 0.26; dialect (Bavarian->Alemannic) 0.45; **A->B 0.46 (herbal only 0.48)**; same dialect other texts 0.58; far dialect 0.71; Latin->Italian 0.78; spelling reform 0.94; 3-letter-swap key 1.23; unrelated 1.29-1.62; full substitution 1.94. Random 6-rule sets improve A->B 1% of the time, as for dialect and same-language pairs (0-1%), vs 13-54% for language, reform, cipher and unrelated pairs | DIALECT RUNG. Grade B (one corpus per rung; content not matched). Kills not fired: split A vs split A is ~0, and A->B < Latin->Italian |
| v30:V-30.F2 | What do the rules look like? | Guided search closes 0.47-0.48 of the A->B gap (herbal 0.60-0.67) vs 0.27-0.35 for dialect and same-language pairs, 0.17-0.25 for Commedia copies, 0.64-0.69 for a spelling reform. Top rules in 4/4 runs: A -chy/-chol/-chor/-or -> B -chedy/-chdy/-edy, and word-initial cth reduced. Edge-only and context-free rules close A->B equally (0.38/0.38), unlike reform/cipher (free 2x edge) | SYSTEMATIC, ENDING-CENTRED, NOT A LETTER KEY. A->B is a dialect-sized but reform-regular shift, dominated by one ending family. Grade B |
| v30:V-30.F3 | Does it predict outside the fitting data? | Rules learned on herbal A->herbal B move unseen A pharma toward unseen B biological (-8%), stars (-4%), hand 3 (-5%); all 300 random rule sets and 146/150 shuffled-rhs sets do worse; German dialect rules transfer the same way (-10%) but not to another dialect (+1.5%) | YES, out-of-sample within the manuscript (grade B). Nothing outside the manuscript is predicted, so nothing is read: NOT cracked |
| v30:V-30.F4 | Within B | B hand 2 vs hand 3 (different sections) sits at null level (0.02) | Grade A (in this metric): B's two main scribes share one system; the A/B split is not a scribe-by-scribe drift |
| v30:V-30.F5 | Direction | A->B 0.48 vs B->A 0.45 (dialect 1.08, reform 1.19, Latin/Italian 1.40) | Nearly symmetric; no sign of a lossy one-way reform. Grade C |
| v30:V-30.F | Overall | - | Currier A and B are two conventions of ONE system at dialect distance, separated mainly by a regular ending change (A -y/-or/-ol vs B -edy/-dy) plus fewer word-initial cth in B. Not two languages, not two cipher keys, not two scribes' noise. Next: run the ladder on IT2a and on a second dialect pair (ReF Swabian/Bavarian) to firm grade B; test whether the -edy rule is line-position or paragraph-position conditioned |

ENHG allo +0.10/+0.12/+0.09, plain null. CATMuS Latin with abbreviation marks +0.25 / -0.11 / +0.14 (ppmi p 0.0002), plain +0.10/+0.01/+0.07. Many p < 0.01 | YES, PARTLY: graphic-unit transcriptions of an arbitrary alphabet DO give a significant positive shape-behaviour r (typically +0.10 to +0.20, best +0.25 on one model), so v25's printed-font null (~0) was too easy a baseline. The non-featural source is mostly the combining/abbreviation marks and minim strokes (small, similar-looking units that all attach after letters), not the allographs |

| v28:V-28.2.V1 | Verdict: other scripts (mean image-combo r over fonts, ppmi / svd / potts) | Ethiopic Amharic (abugida, 208 syllables) +0.17 / +0.08 / +0.06 (p 0.0002), featural layout decomposition +0.16 / +0.13 / +0.25 (p 0.0002); Tigrinya replication image ppmi +0.03 to +0.06, potts +0.05 to +0.07 (p 0.0002-0.09), layout +0.18 / potts +0.25. OCS Cyrillic +0.13 / +0.07 / +0.03 (ppmi p 0.003-0.01); the same text in Glagolitic -0.00 / -0.00 / +0.02 (null); Russian +0.01 / +0.02 / +0.00; Armenian +0.04 / +0.05 / +0.06 (n.s.) | Positive control behaves: the near-featural abugida shows the link, the arbitrary alphabets do not (Glagolitic null on identical behaviour shows shape, not text, carries it; OCS Cyrillic's weak link sits in its composite iotated letters). The Voynich image link exceeds even Ethiopic's on all three models (and its hand link exceeds Ethiopic's layout-featural link on ppmi and potts). Grade A |
| v28:V-28.2.V2 | Verdict: does the EVA segmentation create the effect? | Every EVA character a unit (benches split into c + h): ZL +0.37 / +0.34 / +0.38, IT +0.39 / +0.36 / +0.41. i-runs and e-runs as units: ZL +0.27 / +0.28 / +0.37, IT +0.27 / +0.31 / +0.39. Benched gallows split into ch + gallows: +0.24 / +0.37 / +0.48. Split + 6 BPE chunk merges: +0.22 / +0.34 / +0.48. All p <= 0.003, partial given position +0.20 to +0.48 | NO. The link survives every segmentation tried, merged or split, in both transcriptions; splitting the benches makes ppmi stronger. Grade A |
| v28:V-28.2.V3 | Verdict: can composite (multi-letter) units fake it? | Printed Latin with 10 BPE merges +0.05 / +0.05 / +0.08 (p >= 0.02); 25 merges +0.09 / +0.10 / +0.17 (potts p 0.0002), but at 23 units (V-28.3) ~0 | Composite units sharing letters create a weak link that grows with the number of composites, at most 40% of the Voynich potts value; the Voynich link is strongest when no composites exist (split). Grade B |
| v28:V-28.2.V4 | 15th-16th-c. cipher alphabets / invented scripts | Searched DECODE, HF, Zenodo, web: no openly downloadable transcribed corpus of a 15th-16th-c. cipher or invented script with glyph images found (DECODE needs a login; Rohonc Codex has no public machine transcription found; Copiale is 18th c., transcribed as symbol names with line images only, no per-symbol images) | NOT RUN. Open gap: the strongest untested rival class. Would need a human-made per-symbol image set (e.g. Copiale symbol crops) |

| v28:V-28.3.PL1 | PLANTED positional allographs in printed Latin: the word-final form of the 6 commonest letters made a separate unit, drawn IDENTICALLY (latinprint+pallo6) or in the italic cut (pallo6i); full v25 pipeline | identical: r -0.04 to -0.01 (ppmi/potts, 3 fonts, all p > 0.5); italic: -0.01 to +0.09 (p >= 0.08). The allograph-likeness detector ranks exactly the 6 planted pairs on top (i/i', e/e', s/s', t/t', u/u', a/a') | Positional variants CANNOT make the v25 link: under v25's behaviour (L1 R1 L2 R2 with word edges) a final form and its medial twin have different contexts by construction, so look-alike positional variants add nothing. Detector calibrated (finds the planted pairs) |
| v28:V-28.3.V1 | Verdict: size-matched (23 most frequent units each) | Voynich ZL +0.28 / +0.30 / +0.43, IT +0.29 / +0.26 / +0.44, split +0.37 / +0.34 / +0.38 (all p <= 0.001). Rivals: printed Latin -0.04 / +0.02 / -0.05; Latin BPE25 ~0; CATMuS +0.12 / -0.14 / +0.06 (n.s.); Castilian allographetic -0.02 to +0.17 (mostly n.s.); ENHG +0.04 / 0.00 / +0.03; Ethiopic +0.13 / +0.10 / +0.07 (n.s.); OCS +0.13 / 0.00 / +0.01; Glagolitic, Russian, Armenian ~0. Only rival reaching significance on two models: Castilian Val_S hand C MINIM-split +0.26 / +0.13 / +0.26 (p 0.001-0.04) and hand D minim potts +0.23 (p 0.003) | At equal inventory size the graphic-unit rivals mostly fall to noise; the closest is a minim-stroke segmentation of a Gothic hand, which matches the Voynich on ppmi but reaches 60% of its potts value and half its svd. v25 SURVIVES. Grade A |
| v28:V-28.3.V2 | Verdict: positional variants | Voynich: r is HIGHER among pairs that share word positions (ZL ppmi +0.30, potts +0.50; IT +0.28 / +0.51) than among complementary pairs (+0.23 / +0.35); dropping the 3-10 most allograph-like pairs leaves r unchanged (+0.28-0.30 / +0.43-0.44). Medieval sets: removing the 9-20 KNOWN allograph pairs changes r by <= 0.02 | KILLED as an explanation (both by the planted control and on the data): the Voynich link is not carried by positional variants, and real allographs do not carry the link in medieval hands either. | v34:V-34.1 | Cycle verdict | - | Voynich line-final glyphs do not respond to physical crowding (grade B negative), but the Latin positive control does not respond either, so the test cannot tell 'no abbreviation' from 'abbreviation not driven by room'. Kill rule of idea #1 needs the Latin slope; it is absent |
| v34:V-34.2.1 | POSITIVE CONTROL, same image pipeline: tightness vs last-token abbreviation | abbr_last z -0.65, abbr_last_any -0.30, with length covariate -0.67; halves A +1.15, B -1.39; tight lines 0.445 marks vs roomy 0.525 | Still no abbreviation response in Latin. | v34:V-34.2.2 | What Latin DOES do in tight lines | dens_rest z +6.4 (halves +2.7, +6.1): tight lines are written with wider spacing all along; last word slightly squeezed (comp_last z -2.4) | Latin scribes fill the line by spreading the whole line (justification), not by abbreviating the last word |
| v34:V-34.2.3 | Same features in the Voynich | dens_rest z +0.4 (recto +1.6, verso -1.0); comp_last z +3.8 (recto +3.5, verso +1.4, opposite sign to Latin) | The Latin line-spreading signature is ABSENT: Voynich line density does not track how close the line comes to the margin. Line reach is set by what is written (words per line z +4.4), not by stretching. comp_last does not replicate across halves (artefact-prone, grade C at best) |
| v34:V-34.2.4 | Final glyphs m, g, n, y and last-word length, recto vs verso | all |z| < 1 in verso; recto nglyph_last z -3.1 (pFW 0.01), verso +0.8 | The only half-significant effect (shorter last words in tight recto lines) does not replicate. No glyph behaves like an abbreviation mark under crowding |
| v34:V-34.2 | Cycle verdict | - | Same pipeline, same null: Latin shows a strong layout response (spread spacing) but no abbreviation response; the Voynich shows neither. Grade C: 'later in the line = more a, fewer e'. Would support: same drift in IT2a and on herbal pages with drawings; would kill: it vanishes once word length is matched |
| v34:V-34.3 | Cycle verdict | - | The null on m/g/-am is a powered null: crowding-driven final-m abbreviation in >= 1 in 5 tight lines is excluded (grade B). No Voynich glyph behaves as an abbreviation mark under crowding. But the Latin control never abbreviates more under crowding either, so 'abbreviation' as such is not excluded, only room-driven abbreviation |
| v34:V-34.F1 | Do Voynich line-final glyphs respond to crowding? | 27 features (final m g n y l r s d o, -am -aiin -ain -dy -ey -ol -ar -al -or, length, frequency, hapax, uncertain spaces, gallows): none survives familywise (best |z| 2.1); final m z -0.4, g +0.5, -am -1.1. Planted m-abbreviation in 20% of tight lines is found 75%, at 35% always | NO (grade B negative, powered): m, g and -am are line-end markers independent of the room left. Crowding-driven m-abbreviation in more than ~1 of 5 tight lines is excluded |

Latin scribes here do not abbreviate more when the line is crowded; idea #1's kill rule ('Latin shows the slope') cannot be applied. The abbreviation question stays open in principle |
| v34:V-34.F3 | How do the two scripts fill a line? | Latin: tight lines are spread wider all along (density z +6.4, both halves); last word slightly squeezed (z -2.4). Voynich: density flat (z +0.4; recto +1.6, verso -1.0); reach set by words per line (z +4.4) | Grade B: the Voynich scribe does NOT justify lines by spacing, unlike the Latin control. Lines are composed to length, consistent with v26 line-width filling being content-side |
| v34:V-34.F4 | Greedy wrapping? | next-line first-word length vs tightness: print z -15.1, Latin MSS -2.2, Voynich +0.8 | Grade A (this sample): Voynich lines are not running text wrapped at the margin (physical confirmation of the sealed line) |
| v34:V-34.F5 | Candidate abbreviation glyphs | none respond to crowding. Side result: a left-to-right drift inside lines (a, r, p up; e, k, sh, q down; 19-24/25 pages) that does not grow with crowding | Grade C side finding (would support: same in IT2a and other sections; would kill: disappears with word length matched) |
| v34:V-34.F | Overall | - | Voynich line endings do not respond to physical crowding; no glyph behaves like a crowding-driven abbreviation mark (powered null for m). The Latin control does not abbreviate under crowding either, so this kills 'room-driven abbreviation', not abbreviation. The strongest new contrast is layout: Latin justifies by spacing, the Voynich does not. Not cracked |

### v27: the lines were cards, and the deck was shuffled (4 Oct 2026; `loops/v27_final.txt`)
Every body line is a card; the best order is searched (exact DP / simulated annealing in C) within paragraphs and across the whole book, under a junction scorer trained only on within-line word pairs plus a lexical scorer, without ever seeing the written order. Controls: Latin and Italian herbals (positive), cross-page independent lines (negative), word-shuffled and Markov-resynthesised nulls, planted hidden continuity (also across pages), a two-witness test with ~2,000 random junction hypotheses.

| Run | Method and control | Result | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| v27:V-27.1.1 | POSITIVE Italian herbal (Brumati), lines shuffled within paragraph and re-sequenced | J: adj 0.411 vs chance 0.099, W 73.9; order-blind G 103 vs nulls (F3, F5, cross-page) 88-93, PE 0.735 vs 0.62-0.67, mb 0.465 vs 0.37-0.38 | PASSES: 4x chance recovery, and the hidden order is visible without the written order (G +11, PE +0.07) |
| v27:V-27.1.2 | POSITIVE Latin Isidore XVI-XVII, same | J: adj 0.257 vs 0.164, W 20.6; G 57.6 vs nulls 54.7-56.7, PE 0.538 vs 0.49-0.51 | PASSES weakly: recovery 1.6x chance; order-blind signal small (G +1-3) |
| v27:V-27.1.3 | NEGATIVE independent lines: cross-page (each body line replaced by a body line of another page, same section; LA, IT, ZL, IT2a) | W (S) -0.1 to 1.3; adj 0.10/0.16/0.21/0.20 = chance | Clean: independent cards give no written-order chains |
| v27:V-27.1.4 | NULLS on Voynich: words shuffled in line, middle words shuffled (ends kept), F3 / F5 Markov resyntheses (independent lines) | ZL J: G 61.9-64.3, PE 0.588-0.627, mb 0.370-0.421; L: G 67.6-69.2, PE 0.655-0.664, mb 0.459-0.465 | Null band for the order-blind statistics |
| v27:V-27.1.5 | PLANTED: ZL F3 resynthesis with hidden continuity (first word of line k redrawn from the in-line junction table on the last glyph of line k-1 at rate rho_j; or a word of line k-1 copied into line k at rate rho_l) | rho_j .60: PE(J) 0.685, adj .254; rho_j .30: PE 0.650, adj .227; rho_l .30: mb(L) 0.497, W(L) 27; rho_j=rho_l=.15: adj(S) .274 but PE 0.631, mb 0.477 | Order-blind detection power: junction continuity in >= ~30-60% of joins, or word copying in >= ~30% of joins; weaker plants are invisible without the written order |
| v27:V-27.1.6 | VOYNICH ZL3b real | J: W 0.7, adj .190 (chance .201), G 62.6, PE 0.619, mb 0.384; L: W 9.9 (F3/F5 4.8-5.4 from the short-last-line end effect), G 68.0, PE 0.664, mb 0.464; S: adj .228 | NO hidden order: every order-blind statistic sits inside the null band (PE 0.619 vs 0.588-0.627; plant .30 gives 0.650). The written order carries a small lexical adjacency excess (L W 9.9 vs 4.8-5.4 in resyntheses) but no junction flow (J W 0.7) |
| v27:V-27.1.7 | VOYNICH IT2a real | J: W 1.4, G 61.8, PE 0.618, mb 0.387; L: W 9.7, PE 0.662, mb 0.462 (nulls 0.578-0.629 / 0.644-0.663) | Replicates: nothing beyond the null band |
| v27:V-27.1 | Cycle verdict | - | Re-sequencing works on real prose (Italian 4x chance, Latin 1.6x) and on strong plants, but the Voynich body lines show no better-flowing hidden order inside paragraphs than independent resynthesised lines do. Upper bound: junction continuity in < ~30% of line joins and word copying in < ~30% of joins at this scorer. Grade B negative |
| v27:V-27.2.1 | POSITIVE Latin / Italian herbals: all cards of all pages in one deck | Written hits Latin 27/2219 (chance 0.8; F5 resynthesis 1), Italian 51/3667 (chance 0.9; F5 5); longest recovered chain 2-3 cards; same-page links 5.8% / 3.9% (F5 3.5% / 1.6%, base 0.9% / 0.4%) | Detectable (25-50x chance) but tiny: at book scale the scorer is swamped by spurious high-scoring pairs (path edge mean 5.2 vs written -2.4 in Latin). Whole-book reassembly has almost no power even for prose |
| v27:V-27.2.2 | PLANTED: ZL F3 resynthesis with a hidden order that crosses pages (random permutation of each section's cards; junction redrawn at rho_j, word copied at rho_l along it) | rho .6/.3: 25/3358 hidden links found (chance 1.0); rho .3/.15: 14/3358; stable across restarts 4 / 0 | Found above chance, but only 0.4-0.7% of the hidden chain: a cross-page deck is recoverable only as scattered links, never as chains |
| v27:V-27.2.3 | VOYNICH ZL3b vs nulls (F5 page-topic resynthesis, cross-page cards, words shuffled in line) | Written hits 6/2635 (F5 6, cross 2, wshuf 7); same-page links 3.3% (F5 3.6%, wshuf 3.2%, cross 1.7%; base 0.7%); same-section 43% (base 21%; F5 49%); longest chain 2 | NO book-scale chains: the written order is not re-found beyond the null; links stay on the page and section only as much as page topic predicts (F5 equals real) |

| v27:V-27.2 | Cycle verdict | - | At book scale the method recovers prose and planted orders only as scattered links (25-50x chance, <2%); the Voynich gives no more than its nulls on every statistic. Nothing suggests cards were distributed across pages. Grade B negative at the power stated (low) |
| v27:V-27.3.0 | ARTEFACT check: hypothesis #7 shared witness B's tie-breaking jitter seed | It alone gave z 15.6 (Italian) and 8.3 (Latin) | Caught and excluded: identical tie-breaks make two witnesses 'agree'. Also excluded: hypotheses using penultimate / second words (they overlap witness B) |

Grade B (replicates in IT2a: 9.7 / 1.4) |
| v27:V-27.F | Overall | - | THE DECK WAS NOT SHUFFLED, OR NEVER FLOWED. Lines carry no lost sequence that any order-blind statistic can find, at a power that finds real Italian prose and moderate plants. Combined with v22 (no order beyond the head line): each body line is a self-contained unit, and was so when it was written. Not cracked; nothing read; nothing predicted outside the corpus. Grade C guess: body lines are independent entries (one item per line); would support: line-level units with entry-initial vocabulary on every line (v-b line openers); would kill: a transcription or image correction showing words split across line breaks |
| v33:V-33.1.1 | ECOLOGY PROFILE. Words split stem + ending by 32 corpus-relative rules (fix-1/2/3, mean-position slot rules pos-0.5..0.8, top-K suffix freq-10/25/50/100, Harris successor peak, 20 random suffix inventories) plus 4 v7 slot rules (Voynich alphabet only); presence matrix of the 250 most frequent stems x 30 most frequent endings in a 10,000-token block; connectance, NODF, C-score, Barber modularity Q (BRIM, 6 starts, same optimiser for nulls), stem-degree CV, ending-degree Gini; z against 30 curveball nulls (fixed row and column totals; mixing checked at 5n/20n/60n trades, identical). 2 blocks per corpus: Voynich ZL, IT, Currier A, B; Latin (Isidore), Old Czech (Dalimil), German (Kafka), Italian (Manzoni), Hungarian, Turkish (news), Hebrew (Mishneh Torah); generators: in-word slot trigram, running-text glyph trigram (Markov resynthesis), table-and-grille, each fitted on the Voynich and on Latin; v26 minimal genome | Medians over 32 rules. Languages: connectance 0.05-0.09, zQ 0.8-4.9, C 0.81-0.92, zNODF -1.4 to -11.6 (all ANTI-nested beyond their degree sequences). Voynich ZL/IT/A/B: connectance 0.14-0.15, zQ 16-20, C 0.58-0.60, zNODF -4.7 to -6.0. Voynich-fitted slot / trigram / v26 generators: connectance 0.12-0.14, zQ 14-16, C 0.58-0.62. Latin-fitted slot / trigram: connectance 0.16, zQ 9-10, C 0.60 (Latin itself 0.09, 4.9, 0.85). Table-and-grille: connectance 0.05-0.06, Q 0.75-0.79, zQ ~0 (block-diagonal grid, nothing beyond its degrees) | The Voynich stem x ending grid is twice as full as any language's and three to ten times more modular beyond its degrees; this is exactly where glyph-trigram generators put ANY source, including Latin. No language in the panel is nested beyond its degree sequence: 'nested paradigms' do not show as a z-score with fixed-totals nulls, so nestedness cannot classify here |
| v33:V-33.1.2 | NULL CONTROL 1: stem-ending RE-PAIRING (endings shuffled over tokens, both token marginals kept), same 32 rules; Voynich ZL, Latin, glyph-shuffled Voynich | Median abs z (share with abs z < 2): Latin zNODF 0.68 (0.94), zQ 0.79 (0.95), zC 1.23 (0.70); Voynich zNODF 1.06 (0.72), zQ 1.15 (0.77), zC 2.41 (0.38) | Nestedness and modularity collapse to the null as required. The C-score does not fully collapse on Voynich (token-weighted random pairing is not the presence-level curveball null): zC is unreliable and is down-weighted in the verdicts |
| v33:V-33.1.3 | NULL CONTROL 2: glyphs shuffled inside each Voynich word, then the same rules | zQ 9.3 (Voynich 16), zNODF -1.0, connectance 0.13, C 0.69 | FAILS to collapse fully: shuffled words keep zQ 9. Stem and ending of a shuffled word are drawn from one glyph multiset, so modules form by glyph composition. Modularity z therefore measures stem-ending DEPENDENCE in general, not declension classes; it re-measures the v14 slot dependence (Grade A there) |
| v33:V-33.1.4 | POSITIVE CONTROL: planted inflecting paradigm (150 Voynich-like stems from the slot generator, 3 declension classes x 6 endings taken from frequent Voynich word ends, Zipf stems and endings) replacing 15% or 30% of the tokens of the ZL blocks | Change vs the unplanted block, median over 32 rules x 2 blocks: dNODF +0.24 / +0.32 (share > 0: 0.58), zNODF -0.03 / -1.21, dQ -0.01 / -0.02, connectance -0.005 / -0.012; block-to-block spread of the unplanted text: dNODF 0.73, zNODF 2.7, dQ 0.014, connectance 0.008 | NOT DETECTED by any single metric: the planted paradigm moves nestedness less than the difference between two Voynich blocks. Only connectance drops slightly (plant adds lexical gaps). The ecology tools cannot see a hidden paradigm at 15-30% dilution, so they cannot exclude one in the Voynich. FAIL of the positive control as specified |
| v33:V-33.1.S | Cycle summary | - | The ecology profile separates languages from generators by two numbers, connectance and modularity z, and the Voynich sits with generators. Hungarian <-> Turkish pair up; German <-> Czech; Hebrew nearest Turkish/Czech | The profile tells a language from a generator fitted to that language without error, and partly recovers families (agglutinating pair found; root-pattern Hebrew not distinct). | v33:V-33.3.1 | GAP RATIO (null-free, held out): connectance of the stem x ending grid divided by that of the text's OWN glyph-trigram resynthesis (T) and own in-word slot generator (S), fitted on the whole source; fresh blocks (seed 34) and 132 rules, 100 of them new random rules never used in cycles 1-2. Also raw Barber Q ratio | Languages, median gap ratio vs T (share of rules < 1), new rules: Latin 0.55 (0.98), Czech 0.66 (0.95), German 0.45 (0.97), Italian 0.58 (0.98), Hungarian 0.53 (0.95), Turkish 0.52 (0.98), Hebrew 0.49 (0.97); Q ratio 1.25-1.74. Voynich: ZL 0.95 (0.62), IT 0.97 (0.65), Currier A 1.10 (0.16), B 1.02 (0.40); Q ratio 0.96-1.03. Old rules give the same numbers | Every language leaves 34-55% of the stem x ending cells that its own glyph statistics would fill EMPTY (lexical gaps), and its grid is 25-86% more modular than its resynthesis. The Voynich has NO gaps and NO extra modularity beyond its glyph trigram, in both transcriptions and both Currier languages, on held-out rules and blocks. Grade B, pending the confound tests below |
| v33:V-33.3.2 | POSITIVE CONTROL: Voynich trigram text with planted DECLENSION CLASSES (each stem 1 of 4 classes, each class allows a random half of the endings; disallowed words rejected), on 100% or 50% of tokens | 100%: gap ratio 0.75 (0.98 of rules < 1), Q ratio 1.13; 50%: 0.97-0.99 (0.58-0.78), Q 1.00 | Detected when the restriction covers the whole text (gap ratio drops into the 0.75 range, beside the 8-class Latin), barely at 50%. The ratio is sensitive to lexical gaps, but a partial restriction can hide in Voynich-sized samples |
| v33:V-33.3.3 | CONFOUND CONTROLS: Latin in 8 letter classes (small alphabet); Latin with words > 4 letters contracted to first 2 + last 2 letters (short words, medieval suspension/contraction keeping the ending) | 8 classes: 0.78 (0.89-0.99), Q 1.25. Contracted: 0.90-1.01 (0.49-0.62), Q 1.05-1.15 | A SMALL ALPHABET does not erase the gaps (0.78). HEAVY CONTRACTION does: an abbreviated Latin text is as gap-free as its own trigram, like the Voynich. So 'no lexical gaps' does NOT prove generation: it fits a generator OR a text whose words are reduced to short contracted shells. Grade B for the fact (Voynich = own trigram at the stem x ending level), Grade C for either reading (would support abbreviation: a contracted Latin/Italian/German herbal that also falls in the Voynich cluster with the full profile; would kill abbreviation: contracted texts stay apart from the Voynich on the 6-feature profile) |
| v33:V-33.3.S | Cycle summary | - | Held-out, null-free and calibrated on 7 languages: the Voynich stem x ending grid has none of the lexical gaps every language shows (gap ratio 0.95-1.10 vs 0.45-0.66). Two readings survive: glyph-level generation, or words reduced to contracted shells. Cycle 4 runs the full profile on contracted, truncated, recoded and verbose Latin to separate them |
| v33:V-33.4.1 | KILL TEST of the cycle-2 verdict with the full 6-feature profile (30 curveball nulls, 32 rules, 2 blocks each): Latin and Italian contracted to first 2 + last 2 letters; Latin truncated to 4 letters; Latin in 8 letter classes; Latin in a verbose 2-glyph code from 6 glyphs; planted declension classes at 100% / 50% | Distance to the Voynich blocks: contracted Latin 24.5-25.5, contracted Italian 22.5-23.5, truncated 20.6-21.5, 8 classes 42-44, verbose 45-47; planted declension 100% 13.6-17.4, 50% 9.1-10.9 (Voynich block spread 8.2-10.9). Per-rule family votes: contracted Latin GEN 40/64, contracted Italian 36/64, 8 classes 42/64, truncated 21/64, verbose 14/64 | No recoded or abbreviated Latin/Italian lands in the Voynich cluster. Contraction and small alphabets push a language TOWARD generators on single rules, but not near the Voynich. The 100% declension plant moves out of the Voynich cluster (detected), the 50% plant does not |
| v33:V-33.4.2 | DECISIVE CONTROL: distance from each text to its OWN glyph-trigram and slot generators in the full profile, against its own block-to-block spread (contracted texts get their own generators) | Latin 21.1 vs spread 8.1 (2.6x); contracted Latin 21.8-22.1 vs 6.2 (3.5x); contracted Italian 17.7-18.9 vs 8.6 (2.1-2.2x). Voynich ZL 9.4-10.4 vs 10.5 (0.9-1.0x); B to v26 genome 8.9, A to slot generator 10.6. What separates contracted texts from their generators: zNODF -16.3 / -13.8 vs -1.9 / -3.7 (strong anti-nestedness beyond degrees), while their connectance matches (0.19 vs 0.20; 0.16 vs 0.15) | The abbreviation confound of cycle 3 is KILLED for the full profile: contracted languages lose their lexical gaps but keep a strong anti-nested structure (stems avoid sharing endings with stems of similar degree, i.e. paradigm choice), which their glyph generators lack. The Voynich lacks it too (zNODF -4.7 to -6.0, generators -3.5 to -3.9). Gap ratio for contracted texts: Latin 0.90-1.01, Italian 0.93-1.04; truncated Latin 0.66-0.68, verbose Latin 0.51-0.54 (gaps kept) |
| v33:V-33.4.S | Cycle summary | - | Grade B: at the level of which stems take which endings, the Voynich is what its own glyph statistics produce (distance to its trigram, slot and v26 generators = its block spread, 0.9-1.0x), while every tested language, including contracted Latin and Italian, stays 2.1-3.5x its spread from its own generators. Limits: a paradigm covering 15-50% of tokens is not seen (positive control failed at those doses), so the result says the BULK of the text has no stem-specific ending choice beyond glyph statistics, not that no paradigm exists anywhere |
| v33:V-33.F | THE MORPHOLOGY IS AN ECOSYSTEM (v33, 4 cycles; tools/v33_lib.py, v33_cycle1-4c.py, v33_report1/3/4.py; checkpoints data/v33_ckpt/) | Stem x ending presence grids (250 stems x 30 endings, 10,000-token blocks) under 136 segmentation rules (fixed, positional slot, v7 slot, frequent-suffix, Harris, 120 random inventories); connectance, NODF, C-score, Barber modularity, degree spread vs curveball nulls. Panel: Voynich ZL, IT, Currier A, B; Latin, Old Czech, German, Italian, Hungarian, Turkish, Hebrew; glyph-trigram, in-word slot, table-and-grille generators fitted on the Voynich and on Latin; v26 minimal genome; contracted, truncated, recoded and verbose Latin/Italian; planted paradigms and declension classes | (1) Languages: connectance 0.05-0.09, modularity z 1-5, anti-nested (zNODF -1 to -12); their grids leave 34-55% of the cells their own glyph trigram fills empty (gap ratio 0.45-0.66, 7/7 languages, held-out rules). (2) Voynich: connectance 0.14-0.15, modularity z 16-20, gap ratio 0.95-1.10, nearest to its own trigram / slot / v26 generators on 256/256 rule-block votes and 1,600/1,600 bootstraps, at a distance equal to its block-to-block spread (0.9-1.0x); Latin sits 2.6x its spread from its own generators. (3) Kill tests: contracted Latin/Italian lose their gaps but stay 2.1-3.5x their spread from their own generators (anti-nestedness survives); small alphabet, truncation and verbose coding keep gaps. (4) Controls: re-paired stems/endings collapse NODF and Q to the null (median abs z ~1); glyph-shuffled words keep modularity z 9 (modularity partly measures composition dependence); a 15-30% planted paradigm and a 50% declension restriction are NOT detected, a 100% restriction is | GRADE B: the Voynich stem x ending ecology is that of a text generated from its own glyph statistics: no lexical gaps and no paradigm-choice structure beyond what a glyph trigram or slot generator gives, stable over both transcriptions, Currier A and B and 136 rules; every language tested, abbreviated or not, departs from its own generator by 2-3.5x. GRADE C (lead): the Voynich falls with generators, not with fusional, agglutinating or root-pattern languages; would support = a real abbreviated or shorthand medieval text that also sits at its own generator's distance; would kill = such a text found, or a 30% paradigm becoming detectable with a better metric and found in the Voynich. Not shown: absence of any paradigm (positive control fails below 100%). Nestedness does not classify languages here (all anti-nested) |

A re-lineated copy that kept the exemplar's line-edge forms is ruled out in SB and HA (Grade A for that model; Grade B overall). Exemplar line length per section: none detectable. Leeway guess (C): line-by-line composition on the page (consistent with v6/v10 line-marker and v11 junction results). Would support: line-edge habits respond to physical obstacles (drawings) the way an author, not a copyist, would; would kill: a confirmed eye-skip that drops exactly one line-length of text. Persistent across generations and transcriptions: near_far (edit-1 variants 2+ lines apart, z +7 to +9) and ch/sh runs (CS_switch, z -4.6 to -5.6) | Grade B: the Voynich has page-level structure beyond every forger tried, all of it local copy-and-vary or layout structure |

Voynich: NO topic survivor in any generation; its paragraph-first line is re-used LESS than forged (z -5 to -7) | Grade B: generator-like in KIND (structure of a copy-and-vary procedure with layout rules, not of a text about a subject), but no single known generator suffices; it takes a stack of >= 6 local rules and still leaves near_far and ch/sh runs |
| v21:V-21.F.6 | Working readings | (C) near_far = a scribe varying words seen 2+ lines earlier. (C) ch/sh runs = a pen or table state that persists across words. Would support: runs persist across line breaks at the junction-reset rate; would kill: they vanish once page ch/sh rates are matched | Not cracked. Nothing here predicts anything outside the corpus |

C: its direction points backwards (as if each word were spelled from its end), a weak but consistent sign also produced by the glyph chain |
| v23:V-23.F.3 | Word level | Lag-2 word arrow null in Voynich (z 0.7 / 1.6 at 35k tokens) vs languages z 13-31; function-word stripping cuts languages to z 3.0-11.6 (residue survives). | v23:V-23.F.4 | Line and paragraph level | Only asymmetries are layout (paragraph-initial line, short last lines, long line-initial words); the 'introduction-then-reuse' signal was killed by dropping the first line | No discourse arrow detectable (upper bound: none at the size of the language residues) |
| v23:V-23.F.5 | Neural predictor | Languages 10/10 runs forward-easier (+0.005 to +0.046 bits/glyph); Voynich -0.0075 / +0.0045 (ZL), +0.0136 / +0.0027 (IT) | C: weaker, seed-unstable arrow. Single neural fits need seed replication (per-page z ignores model variance) |
| v23:V-23.F.V | Overall | | The text 'knows' time only inside words and across word junctions, where the arrow is stronger than in any language and points slightly backwards; between words it is close to time-symmetric, more than content words of five languages. Not cracked; nothing predicts outside the corpus |

| v35:V-35.2.V | Cycle 2 verdict | Voynich +0.28 / +0.30 / +0.43 (23 units, no diacritics). Like-for-like (no marks, ~23 units): Tengwar consonants +0.08 to +0.20, syllabics +0.09 to +0.35 (hand), Borg <= +0.18, Shavian <= +0.16, Copiale / Deseret / Cherokee ~0. Only the planted shape-family cipher, scored with the strokes used to build it, reaches Voynich level | No real invented or cipher script reaches the Voynich on all three models; the Voynich is at the top of the scale even against Tolkien's featural Tengwar once Tengwar's vowel marks are removed. Grade A for the scale, B for the ranking against Tengwar (one text, one mode) |
| v35:V-35.3.1 | INDISTINGUISHABILITY of look-alike glyphs (new statistic). Corpus split into alternating 400-word blocks; PPMI context rows per half; I(a,b) = cross-half cos(a,b) / sqrt(cos(a,a') cos(b,b')), 1 = as alike as a glyph is to itself. Mean I over the 10 most shape-similar pairs; null = random pairs with each glyph replaced by one within +-2 frequency ranks (5,000 draws). Calibration: planted homophone-family cipher (must be ~1), Hangul (featural, distinct jamo) | voy (23 units, hand): top-10 I 0.81 vs freq-matched 0.36 (p 0.0002); voy_it (23 units, hand): top-10 I 0.82 vs freq-matched 0.34 (p 0.0002); plant_family (82 units, hand): top-10 I 0.73 vs freq-matched 0.43 (p 0.0008); hangul (38 units, hand): top-10 I 0.37 vs freq-matched 0.23 (p 0.0056); tengwar (34 units, hand): top-10 I 0.26 vs freq-matched 0.20 (p 0.0716); shavian (48 units, hand): top-10 I 0.29 vs freq-matched 0.16 (p 0.0020); cree (85 units, hand): top-10 I 0.17 vs freq-matched 0.12 (p 0.0268); borg (27 units, hand): top-10 I 0.23 vs freq-matched 0.19 (p 0.1944); copiale (84 units, hand): top-10 I 0.15 vs freq-matched 0.22 (p 0.9746). Voynich top pairs: cfh/cph 0.93, cph/cth 0.90, f/p 0.94, ch/sh 0.91, cfh/ckh 0.80, ckh/cth 0.64 | The Voynich behaves like the PLANTED HOMOPHONE CIPHER (0.81 vs 0.73), not like any featural script (Hangul 0.37, Tengwar 0.26, Shavian 0.29, syllabics 0.17) or real cipher (Borg 0.23, Copiale 0.15). Its most similar glyphs are near-interchangeable in context. Replicates in IT (0.82). Grade A for the measurement, B for the reading |

| v35:V-35.3.V | Cycle 3 verdict | Invented featural scripts make similar shapes behave SIMILARLY but distinctly; a homophone-family cipher makes them behave IDENTICALLY. The Voynich has both signatures: identical-behaving twins in the gallows / bench family and a graded featural link elsewhere | New, falsifiable reading (grade C): the gallows set (k~t, p~f, and the benched forms) and ch~sh are near-equivalents (homophone-like or free/positional variants of a few underlying signs), while the rest of the inventory is featurally graded. Would support: k/t and p/f substitutable within the same words across the corpus beyond the frequency-matched rate; would kill: a corpus slice (e.g. a single scribe or quire) where k and t contexts diverge as much as Hangul jamo pairs |

| v41:V-41.2.V | Cycle verdict | In all 9 groups x 2 transliterations the 11 traits co-move along the A->B direction inside one hand (S z +4.0 to +10.7 except hand-2 bio z 1.7-1.8 and small pharma z 2.6-2.7). The A->B orientation is special: random sign patterns are as coherent in <= 0.7% of draws for 13 of 18 runs. S/R 0.12-0.43, at or above real dated German across texts (0.08-0.18), while the German within-one-manuscript floor is 0. The held-out order beats the shuffled-trait null only modestly (z +1.0 to +2.9). Word-initial benched gallows, the class feature of v36, does NOT take part (-0.43 to +0.40, sign unstable): bench loss is a between-group jump, endings and e-load drift within hands | Grade B: every hand carries a one-dimensional, transcription-robust A->B axis of spelling habits from page to page (a within-hand version of the A/B ladder). Whether that axis is time or subject is not settled here; cycle 3 checks it against the physical book |

| v41:V-41.3.V | Cycle verdict | Two different behaviours. In B (hands 2 and 3, both transliterations) the clock is shared by the two sides of a leaf and by conjugate leaves (B_all leaf z -3.2/-3.4, conjugates -2.6/-2.4; hand-2 bio conjugates -3.0/-3.1; hand-3 stars leaf -2.5/-2.5): a bifolio is one moment of the drift, as a writing-batch clock predicts. In hand-1 A there is no bifolio clustering (z -0.1 to +1.6), but the clock rises with foliation (herbal rho +0.33 ZL / +0.31 IT). Hand 2: inner bifolios less B-like than outer (depth rho -0.32/-0.34; bio -0.55/-0.61, few bifolios) and the second leaf of a pair less B-like in 73% of 48 pairs | Grade B: in B the drift clock is a bifolio property (fits the v8 bifolio writing unit, now for spelling habits not just vocabulary). Grade C: the hand-1 clock runs along the binding order (cycle 4 tests it); would support: the rise holds within quires and only for the A->B orientation; would kill: carried only by between-quire differences or by any random trait combination |

Merging by the planted key restores the text exactly (checked: token-identical), so the question is how far the split moved it | Latin herbal letters tgpba: h21 0.84 -> free 0.84 / pos 0.83, ttr 0.41 -> free 0.50 / pos 0.47, zipf -0.86 -> free -0.86 / pos -0.85, arrow 47 -> free 49 / pos 49, gap 0.56 -> free 0.59 / pos 0.60, pLI 0.57 -> free 0.39 / pos 0.44, pG 0.17 -> free 0.30 / pos 0.26, auc 0.99 -> free 0.98 / pos 0.99 // Alemannic German letters svubt: h21 0.79 -> free 0.79 / pos 0.78, ttr 0.23 -> free 0.29 / pos 0.27, zipf -0.97 -> free -0.96 / pos -0.96, arrow 1 -> free 0 / pos 2, gap 0.46 -> free 1.65 / pos 1.84, pLI 0.51 -> free 0.37 / pos 0.46, pG 0.17 -> free 0.23 / pos 0.24, auc 1.00 -> free 1.00 / pos 1.00 // Italian herbal letters lgmvo: h21 0.81 -> free 0.82 / pos 0.81, ttr 0.29 -> free 0.38 / pos 0.35, zipf -0.90 -> free -0.90 / pos -0.90, arrow 232 -> free 208 / pos 217, gap 0.60 -> free 0.63 / pos 0.63, pLI 0.48 -> free 0.32 / pos 0.34, pG 0.37 -> free 0.53 / pos 0.52, auc 0.99 -> free 0.98 / pos 0.99 | The battery IS sensitive to a twin split: a Voynich-matched split lowers P(language) by 0.10-0.16, raises P(generator) by 0.07-0.16 and raises types per 10k by 0.06-0.09 in all three languages (German gap ratio jumps 0.46 -> 1.65). It does NOT erase the frequency arrow (Latin 47 -> 49, Italian 232 -> 208). Merging by the planted key restores everything exactly. Arrow 0-1 (ZL), 3-11 (IT2a, base 7); gap ratio 0.93-1.01 throughout (languages 0.56-0.79). Caveat: index-driven merges of distinct letters can also raise P(lang) in languages (Latin thr 0.6 +0.12, German thr 0.5 +0.21), so P(lang) moves of +-0.1 are within method noise | NO. With the data-driven merges the Voynich moves, if anywhere, TOWARD the generator class, while every planted language moves back to language by the same procedure. The twin structure is real (planted-band I) but its collapse does not uncover a language-like text. Grade A (negative), held out and in two transcriptions |

| v39:V-39.3.V | Cycle-3 verdict: can ANY merge (not just the twins) make the Voynich language-like? | Planted Latin: blind random merging finds held-out survivors (4/12, including the exact planted merge a=#4 alone, pLI 0.39 -> 0.57); the score also rewards merges that just fold rare letters, so the search is sensitive but not specific. Voynich: 260 random merge sets, none beats the unmerged text on discovery by more than +0.06 (best S -0.19 vs -0.25, max P(lang or conlang) 0.26), 0/12 survive on held-out ZL and 0/12 on IT2a; sets with more twin pairs score slightly LOWER (rho -0.12) | NO merge of 1-8 unit pairs, twin or not, makes the Voynich language-like on held-out text, while the same search lifts a planted-split Latin. The generator signature is not hidden by glyph splitting of this size. Grade A (negative; 1-8 pairs, units >= 100 tokens) |

| v42:V-42.1.V | Cycle verdict | (1) The v31 'generator' label is not specific to mechanical generators: in the clean world 65% (collapsed) / 61% (bigram scheme) of 31 surviving random classifiers call the CS a GENERATOR (Voynich 87%), while the raw stroke scheme reads as GIBBERISH (45%) / GEN (32%). Single-model posteriors depend on the unit scheme (raw -> GIBB 0.70; col -> LANG 0.68; sb -> GEN 0.48). (2) At the CS's own OCR noise (n18 world) the classifiers weaken (LOCO 0.68, 6 survivors) and neither CS nor Voynich GEN posterior beats the label-permutation null (p 0.32 / 0.17). (3) Hub test: the CS is nearer the Voynich than any of 123 training texts, though the Voynich attracts no training corpus | Grade B: a long meaningless book by one hand lands in the 'generator' region the Voynich occupies, so v31's GEN verdict cannot separate 'mechanical generator' from 'long asemic writing'. Grade C: the Voynich's nearest real neighbour is the Codex Seraphinianus; would support: a second, independent long asemic book (hand-transcribed) also lands next to it; would kill: an independent long asemic transcription that lands with short gibberish or languages |

| v42:V-42.2.V | Cycle verdict | (1) Per metric the Voynich leaves the language range on 7-9 of 13-15 LONG metrics; the CS leaves it on the same side on 6-8 of them (no lexical gaps / gap ratio V 1.05, CS 0.92-0.98, languages 0.81; low h2 and h3 ratios; strong in-word slot order and position-MI; glyph arrow V 2.41 = CS 2.42, languages 1.86, generators 2.23; line-initial and line-final effects). Over 50-100 corpora only Chinese Pinyin ties the CS for the most shared departures (line metrics dropped). The CS is the nearest group median on 7 of 15 metrics in both worlds. (2) With a 6th class ASEMIC trained only on CS volumes 1 and 2 (held-out volume recognised 2/2 in every run), the Voynich is absorbed at the 3000-token scale: ASEMIC 0.93-0.98 with line metrics, 0.64-0.80 without, A and B both; 0 of 44-91 held-out training corpora and 0 of 3 never-trained odd texts (Martian glossolalia, Lingua Ignota, Steganographia; ASEMIC <= 0.01) are absorbed. At 250 tokens it is a three-way split (ASEMIC 0.26-0.74, GEN, GIBB). (3) Weakness: battery-level LOCO among the four or five base classes is poor (0.29-0.45), so the base classes are not well separated by these 13-15 metrics; the ASEMIC result rests on the CS being a tight, distinct class | Grade B: on the classic battery the Voynich's profile is that of the one long asemic book available, more than of any generator, cipher, language or short gibberish; this holds without line metrics and in noise-matched form. Grade C: 'the Voynich is a long asemic book' as a class claim (n = 1 reference book, machine-read at 18% error); would support: another long one-hand asemic text joins the class; would kill: a mechanical generator or a long meaningful text in a minim script (stroke transliteration) is absorbed by ASEMIC as well |

| v42:V-42.3.V | Cycle verdict | Calibration first: the planted hardening (0 -> 40% slot-generator words along Isidore) is caught by the GEN-posterior trend (rho +0.23, p 0.001) and rigidity (+0.20) but NOT by the 6-trait index (-0.02; planted generator words raise TTR and lower position-MI), so only the GEN trend is a calibrated hardening detector. Its false-positive rate is not small: 1 of 6 long language corpora (German Lite, a multi-work compilation) gives GEN rho +0.18 (p 0.002), and single traits drift in 4 of 6 languages (rigidity -0.27 to -0.33) -> treat |rho| < 0.3 as noise. Result: the Codex Seraphinianus does NOT drift toward generator statistics; it drifts AWAY (GEN rho -0.31, p < 0.001 over 442 windows; volume 1 GEN 0.34 -> volume 2 0.18; no trend inside either volume, -0.01 / -0.08). The Voynich shows no GEN trend in folio order (n18 -0.06, clean 0.00; Currier A -0.09, B 0.00, hand 1 -0.09) and B is no more generator-like than A (0.34 -> 0.31 n18; 0.27 -> 0.18 clean). Short gibberish writers do not harden from their first to their last 100 tokens (GEN up in 20/30, Wilcoxon p 0.40; the language control shows a comparable shift, p 0.003, so this paired test is uncalibrated) | Grade B (negative): the 'habits harden into a system' hypothesis is killed for the one long asemic book measured -- Serafini's text is generator-like from its first pages and becomes less so; the Voynich likewise has no hardening trend. Grade C parallel: in both books the second 'dialect' (CS volume 2, Currier B) is the less generator-like one; would support: the same A->B direction in another long asemic text; would kill: the CS V1/V2 difference reverses under a hand transcription (OCR artefact) |

| v42:V-42.4.8 | Direction of the top discriminators, F3 junction forgery, seed 0 (page means real vs forged) | CS: rare-word re-use on a page 0.029 vs 0.009 (3.3x), any re-use 0.18 vs 0.13, page hapax 0.74 vs 0.80, near-repeats 2+ lines apart 0.11 vs 0.06, line glyph-length SD 0.24 vs 0.34. Voynich: rare-word re-use 0.019 vs 0.0195 (none), any re-use 0.24 vs 0.22, near-repeats 0.28 vs 0.25, line glyph-length SD 0.24 vs 0.29 | The CS has a page-topic signal (rare words recur on their page, as in the Latin and Italian herbals, v21); the Voynich has none. Both share the line-width signal |
| v42:V-42.4.V | Cycle verdict | Forgery detectability. Junction forger F3: Voynich 0.94/0.95 clean and 0.91/0.94 at 18% noise, CS 0.99/1.00 (raw and collapsed), Latin herbal 0.99 clean and 0.97 at 18% noise, Italian herbal 0.98 (ridge/boosted; forgery-vs-forgery null 0.42-0.58 everywhere). Slot forger F4 and the uncalibrated kitchen F6 catch everything at 0.95-1.00. The CS is caught by page topic (rare-word re-use 3.3x the forgery) plus line width; the Voynich by line width and local copying, never by topic. Noise does not explain the gap: the Voynich at 18% noise is still the hardest text to tell from its forgery | Grade B: on the v21 test the long asemic book behaves like a meaningful herbal (topic re-use), NOT like the Voynich. The Voynich remains the only text here whose pages carry no topic: its lack of page topic is not a generic property of long meaningless writing. Grade C: Serafini re-used invented 'names' within a page, the Voynich scribe did not; would support: a hand transcription of CS caption pages shows the same rare-word bursts; would kill: the bursts come from OCR duplicates (repeated word images) |
| v42:V-42.F.1 | Scale | 4,067 v31 feature samples (clean, n09, n18 worlds; 3 CS unit schemes); 1,071 battery blocks at 3000 and 250 tokens (15 metrics incl. v33 gap ratio and v23 arrows); 3,423 100-token windows in writing order; 7 corpora x 3 forgers x 2 seeds forgery runs; ~3,300 random classifiers + permutation nulls | - |
| v42:V-42.F.2 | Controls | LOCO on held-out training corpora 0.76 clean / 0.70 n09 / 0.68 n18 (MAGIC 10/10 always); permuted labels: 0 random survivors (vs 6-31). ASEMIC class: held-out CS volume recognised 8/8 runs; 0 of 44-91 held-out training corpora and 0 of 3 odd never-trained texts absorbed; kill test with 4 meaningful texts and 4 generators rewritten in Gothic minim strokes (+/- 18% noise): ASEMIC 0.00 for all (0/8 absorbed). Planted hardening caught by the GEN trend (rho +0.23, p 0.001). Forgery-vs-forgery AUC 0.42-0.58 | Calibrated, except that the battery-level base classifier is weak (LOCO 0.29-0.45) and the hardening trend has false positives (1 of 6 languages) |
| v42:V-42.F.3 | Is 'generator-like' specific to generators? | In the clean world 65% (repeats collapsed) / 61% (bigram scheme) of 31 surviving random classifiers call the CS a GENERATOR (Voynich 87%); the raw stroke scheme reads gibberish 45% / generator 32%. At the CS's own noise level classification weakens and neither GEN posterior beats its label-permutation null | Grade B: v31's 'generator' verdict does not separate a mechanical generator from long one-hand asemic writing |
| v42:V-42.F.4 | Nearest neighbour (hub test) | The Voynich is the nearest neighbour of 0/123 training corpora, yet the nearest object to the CS in all 3 unit schemes (rank 0-1), apart from the Voynich's own v21 forgeries. CS collapsed -> Voynich 3.8 (clean scaling), closer than all 123 corpora; Martian, Lingua Ignota, Steganographia rank 86-116 | Grade B: of 130+ texts, the Voynich's closest real neighbour is the Codex Seraphinianus |
| v42:V-42.F.5 | Classic battery | The Voynich leaves the language range on 7-9 of 13-15 metrics; the CS leaves it on the same side on 6-8 (no lexical gaps, gap ratio V 1.05 / CS 0.92-0.98 / languages 0.81; low h2, h3; slot rigidity and position-MI; glyph arrow V 2.41 = CS 2.42 vs languages 1.86, generators 2.23; line effects). Only Chinese Pinyin ties the CS for shared departures. ASEMIC class absorbs the Voynich at 3000 tokens (0.64-0.98; A and B both), splits at 250 tokens (0.26-0.74) | Grade B: on the classic battery the Voynich's profile is that of the one long asemic book, more than of any generator, cipher, conlang, language or short gibberish |
| v42:V-42.F.6 | Hardening | The CS does NOT drift toward generator statistics: it drifts away (GEN rho -0.31 over 442 windows; volume 1 0.34 -> volume 2 0.18; flat inside volumes). The Voynich has no trend in folio order (-0.06 / 0.00), A -> B is flat or slightly away from GEN. Short gibberish writers do not harden within 200-480 tokens (p 0.40; the paired test itself is uncalibrated) | Grade B negative: 'habits harden into a system' is killed for the CS; generator-likeness is there from the first pages |
| v42:V-42.F.7 | Forgery detectability (v21) | Junction forger: Voynich 0.94 (0.91 at 18% noise), CS 0.99, Latin herbal 0.99 (0.97 noised), Italian herbal 0.98. The CS is caught by page topic (rare words recur on their page 3.3x the forgery) plus line width; the Voynich has no topic signal (0.019 vs 0.0195) | Grade B: here the asemic book behaves like a meaningful herbal, NOT like the Voynich. The Voynich's topic-free pages are not a generic trait of long meaningless writing |
| v42:V-42.F.8 | Working readings | (C) The Voynich belongs to the class 'long one-hand asemic book' rather than 'mechanical generator': would support: a second, independently transcribed long asemic book joins the ASEMIC class and the Voynich; would kill: a long mechanical generator or a long meaningful minim-script text is absorbed by ASEMIC. (C) Both books have a second 'dialect' that is less generator-like (CS volume 2, Currier B); would kill: the CS V1/V2 difference reverses under hand transcription. (C) Serafini re-used invented names on a page and the Voynich scribe did not; would kill: OCR duplicates explain the CS bursts | Not cracked. Nothing here predicts text outside the corpus |

Voynich: faint diffuse link z ~3 (4 networks, 2 transliterations), no held-out prediction (0.49), no twin effect, no nameable trait | Grade B: the Voynich text does not describe its drawings as a real herbal does. | v40:V-40.2 | Cycle verdict | - | No twin choice depends on the glyph above (the 'avoid tall under tall' aesthetic is absent; the same test finds a planted one at 6% of slots). ch/sh is a POSITIONAL alternation: sh at paragraph and line openings, ch further along the line, independent of word identity (A measurement, two transcriptions). k/t persists along the line and leans to t in opening lines. Grade B: these twins are position-conditioned variants, not meaning-bearing letters |

Size = a copy rule acting on ~20% of slots (planted 10% gives 0.007) |

Strict base with LAYOUT (ZL): 0.011 (z 9.6), different frame 0.011 (z 9.5), 1-5 glyphs 0.017 | Same decaying persistence, but it stops at the line end (the line's opening rule takes over: sh at the start of a line, cycle 2) |
| v40:V-40.4.4 | benched vs plain, p/f, cth/ckh | benched: agreement 0.814 = null 0.814 (ZL), 0.811 = 0.811 (IT), all gains <= 0.002; p/f: agreement = null, gains <= 0; cth/ckh (ZL): 0.020 (z 1.8), distance 6-15 0.054 (z 3.8, n 152) | Benching does not persist; p/f does not; cth/ckh is too small to tell |
| v40:V-40.4 | Cycle verdict | - | k/t and ch/sh carry a short-range 'hand habit': once the scribe has written t (or sh), the next t/k (sh/ch) within a few words leans the same way, in different words, beyond layout and page, fading within ~30 glyphs; for ch/sh it resets at the line start. This is the signature of a writing habit (motor or stylistic persistence), not of meaning, which would follow the word. Grade B (both transcriptions, planted calibration) |
| v40:V-40.F1 | Does the word decide the twin (meaning)? | Unique held-out gain of word identity beyond the neighbouring glyphs: k/t -0.003 / -0.005 bits, ch/sh -0.002 / -0.004 (ZL / IT2a); benched +0.014 / +0.013. Meaning control (Caesar c/g, n/m): +0.11 / +0.12. Context control (DTA long s, r rotunda): CTX carries all of it (0.20-0.30 bits), word identity 0 | NO for k/t and ch/sh: the same word-shape takes either twin with no word-specific preference (A measurement, both transcriptions). Benching is a context rule with a small word residue (B) |
| v40:V-40.F2 | Does the page layout decide it (the glyph above, tall-under-tall)? | Search of ~1,000 'line above' hypotheses per pair: no survivor in any pair or transcription (best inside the search null); planted tall-under-tall found at 6% of slots, missed at 3%. Ink load (cycle 3): no effect, powered at 10-20% | NO: no aesthetic stacking rule, no pen-load rule (B negative, power stated) |
| v40:V-40.F3 | Does POSITION decide it? | ch/sh: the same word-shape on the same page is written sh with odds x3.4 / x3.3 in a paragraph's first line, x2.4 / x2.2 in a line's first word, x0.59 / x0.61 in the right half (CIs exclude 1; search survivors confirmed on held-out pages, z 7-9). k/t: t x1.8 / x1.6 in the first line, x1.3 in the right half. Benched: no position effect. Long-s control search: no survivor | YES for ch/sh (A measurement, B reading: a positional alternation like an initial/medial allograph rule, but keyed to the start of the line and paragraph, not of the word); weakly for k/t (B) |
| v40:V-40.F4 | Does the previous choice decide it (hand habit)? | k/t: the last k/t of the line predicts the next (agreement 0.63 vs 0.60, z 10-11, both transcriptions), in different words (0.017-0.019 bits), fading from 0.022-0.026 bits within 5 glyphs to 0 beyond 30, weakly across the line break; survives a base with all layout features (z 13). ch/sh: same (z 10-12; survives layout base z 9.5), but resets at each new line. Benched and p/f: no persistence. Planted copying in 5% of slots is found | YES: a short-range persistence that is not word copying and not layout (A measurement, B reading: a scribal habit or 'run' of the pen/style) |
| v40:V-40.F5 | Overall: what decides each twin? | k/t: mostly unpredictable (10% of its entropy explained); what helps is local glyph context, a run-persistence of the previous k/t, and a lean to t in opening lines. ch/sh: position in the paragraph and line (sh opens, ch follows) plus the same run-persistence, reset per line. Benched vs plain: neighbouring glyphs (like long s). p/f, cth/ckh: nothing found (n 1,700 each) | The twins behave like calligraphic/positional variants written by habit and position, NOT like meaning-bearing letters (Caesar control) and NOT like an aesthetic page rule. Reading (grade C): k~t and ch~sh are one underlying unit each, the visible form chosen by line position and a decaying stylistic run. Would support: in a hand-checked page sample, sh/ch and t/k runs align with visible changes of pen angle or ductus; the same word-shape switching form at the paragraph opening in held-out folios. Would kill: a scribe or section where the same word-shape keeps one form regardless of position and run, or a meaning-bearing minimal pair (k-word and t-word in complementary semantic contexts, e.g. different illustration sections, beyond frequency) |
| v40:V-40.F6 | Limits | Text offsets stand in for physical x (lines are left-justified; image geometry only for 25 pages); the search is blind to rules acting on < ~5% of slots; p/f and cth/ckh have too few slots; the cycle 1 null (within frame x hand) let page-level drift into PEN/LAYOUT and was replaced by frame x page in cycles 2 and 4 | Not cracked. No new prediction outside the corpus besides the would-support lines above |
| v43:V-43.F1 | Controls | Planted German / Latin islands (600-2,400 tokens, transliterated into Voynich units) found at their place (scan p <= 0.001; German argmax inside 11/12; 98.7% of random survivors); contiguous 12-page plant recovered on held-out lines (recall 0.92-1.0, precision 1.0). Uniform trigram and forger text: no island (2.3% of survivors significant; no held-out gain). Real-language windows: 10 languages, but the classifier band is wide (CATMuS Latin MSS mean 0.32) | Detector works for contiguous islands >= 600 tokens; scattered page-sized islands (12 pages) are NOT recoverable (recall <= 0.5): upper bound only |
| v43:V-43.F2 | Is there an island? | Yes, a relative one: biological quire M, hand 2, ~f75-f84 (core f76r-f82v), ~2,200-4,900 tokens (6-14% of the text). Scan p < 0.0005 at all sizes; found by 83% (ZL) / 79% (IT2a) of 298 random detectors, 69-91% of them in quire M; holds on held-out lines (z +1.7 to +4.6; random survivors z +3.9/+13.2). No section, quire, hand, Currier language or bifolio is significant as a whole once contiguity and the number of groups are corrected | Grade B: the most language-like stretch of the book is BB quire M, hand 2 (consistent with v31's BB section effect, now located and sized) |
| v43:V-43.F3 | Is the island language? | No. On unseen lines it sits at the bottom edge of the language band (0.23-0.34 vs tau 0.33; planted German 0.41-0.51; best windows 0.36-0.62 vs planted 0.59-0.74), barely above uniform trigram text (band reached by 52% vs 36% of detectors). Gap ratio 1.03 (random Voynich stretches 0.94-1.07; languages 0.65-0.87): no lexical gaps, like the rest; 0/28 Voynich windows pass both classifier and gap. Its feature shift is not toward language (17/32 features, projection p 0.26; planted German 26/32): it is a repetition texture (fewer types and endings, more repeated words) | Grade A negative: no window of >= 1,200 tokens is language-like on calibrated tests. Grade B: the island is a repetition texture, not a language island |
| v43:V-43.F4 | Working readings | (B) The text is uniform in kind; quire M / hand 2 is the extreme of a repetition gradient, not a different source. (C) Guess: hand 2 in quire M filled pages with a smaller word stock (would support: the same island in other repetition measures and in the M pages of other hands; would kill: an M-like island in another hand with no repetition shift). Limits: islands smaller than ~600 tokens or scattered over single pages are below resolution; the frequency arrow and forgery AUC have no power at window size | Not cracked |
| v43:V-43.1.1 | Score: v31 classifier P(language)+P(conlang) per 100-token chunk, window = mean of 6/12/24 consecutive chunks (600/1,200/2,400 tokens, step 100). Control: 10 real languages (Isidore, CATMuS Latin MSS, Alemannic, Bavarian, Italian MSS, Old Czech, Dante, Caesar, Anglo-Saxon, Turkish), classifier refitted without each | Language windows: median 0.55-0.78, but CATMuS Latin MSS only 0.32 (min 0.19). 5th percentile 0.33 / 0.26 / 0.29 (W 6/12/24); 75-85% of language windows above 0.5 | Control passes only loosely: the classifier band is wide, so tau alone cannot certify a window as language |
| v43:V-43.1.2 | Uniform generator controls (glyph-trigram resynthesis of the whole MS; global junction forger), same slots | No significant island (scan p 0.09-0.64 trigram, 0.05-0.44 forger). But trigram windows reach 0.43 / 0.34 (W6/W12), above the language 5th percentile | Uniform text gives no island (pass). The uniform-generator maximum is used as a stricter ceiling |
