| v45:V-45.F | THE RESIDUAL IS THE MESSAGE (6 cycles; tools/v45_*.py; loops/v45_cycle1-6.txt, v45_final.txt): v26 rule model cross-fitted; residual = which allowed alternative was chosen, per word and per page (observed minus rule-expected). Controls: rule-only generators, a richer misfit generator, Latin and Gerard text pushed through planted verbose codes in the same rules | Excess surprise V +0.19 bits/word = misfit generator +0.24 (codes +0.44 to +1.26); residual repeats within bifolio/quire (z 7-10, both transcriptions; generators 0); no drawing tracking (residual below surface, random search held-out z -1.4; plant discovery +3.8); residual stream not language-like; PIT surplus = opening lines (42% off-table words vs 26%) | Grade B: residual = misfit plus writing-unit drift, not a message; Grade C: openings hold entry-specific content. Blind to proportional (steganographic) or letter-level codes. Not cracked |
| v45:V-45.1.0 | RULE MODEL. v26 minimal genome (rich junction key, line-initial chain, paragraph drift; section x position tables) plus backoff to the last-unit junction table and section unigram (weights 0.3/0.3/0.4 tuned on V cross-fit), slot-template escape for words outside the fitting half. 2-fold cross-fit by pages: fitted on one half, every word of the other half scored. Per word: surprisal, model entropy, rank and PIT of the chosen word among the allowed alternatives; per page: expected count of every type (residual usage = (observed - expected)/sqrt(expected)) | V ZL 11.44 bits/word (unsmoothed genome 12.26); 30% of tokens lie outside every table (fold-new or table-absent words) | Rule model built; identical pipeline for every corpus |
| v45:V-45.1.1 | CORPORA through the same pipeline. Negatives: GEN0, GEN1 = the v26 minimal genome's own output on the V skeleton (rules, no message); PL = the richer v26 planted genome (copy-and-vary, ch/sh runs, m/g quota: rules the rule model lacks, so misfit without message). Positives: Latin Isidore XVI-XVII pushed through a planted verbose encoding with the same Voynich-fitted rules: LAw one Voynich word per Latin word (Latin types dealt into 400 buckets of Voynich types), LAl one word per Latin letter (26 buckets); the rule model picks the word inside the bucket. Gerard Herball page text the same way (GEw, GEl) | 202 V pages / 34,684 words; IT2a 201 / 34,235; GEN 34.5k; PL 34.7k; LAw 98 pages / 19.2k; LAl 66.6k; GEw 144 pages; GEl 144 pages | - |
| v45:V-45.1.2 | HOW HARD ARE THE CHOICES PUSHED? Excess surprise = surprisal minus model entropy at that slot (bits/word, in-table words); PIT histogram (10 bins) and KS distance from uniform | V +0.19 (KS 0.056; PIT bins rise to 0.142 at 0.8-0.9); IT2a +0.21 (0.054); GEN0/GEN1 -0.38/-0.39 (0.081/0.086); PL (misfit, no message) +0.24 (0.034); LAl (letter code) +0.44 (0.066); LAw (word code) +1.26 (0.210, PIT piles at the improbable end) | The Voynich deviates from its rules exactly as much as a richer generator with no message deviates from a poorer model (+0.19 vs +0.24); a crude bucket code pushes 2-6x harder. Upper bound: any message carried by crude forcing of choices costs <= ~0.2 bits/word of excess; a code that picks in proportion to the rule probabilities (perfect steganography) is invisible to this measure |
| v45:V-45.1.3 | DOES THE RESIDUAL REPEAT WITH SHARED LABELS? Mean cosine of residual usage for page pairs sharing a label minus pairs not sharing it, within section (section: across all; illustration type: within Currier language); null = labels permuted within stratum (1000x). Compared with raw tf-idf. V and IT2a; GEN0/GEN1/PL negatives | V raw: section z +10.9, hand +4.1, quire +10.3, bifolio +12.8. V residual: section -15.6 (removed; same in every corpus, -15 to -22: subtracting a section model), hand +2.0, quire +7.4, bifolio +9.6, illus -11.2. IT2a residual: hand +2.1, quire +7.6, bifolio +10.1. GEN0/GEN1/PL residual: hand +0.4 to +0.7, quire -0.0 to +0.2, bifolio -0.5 to -0.2 | The Voynich residual keeps what the rules cannot make: pages of one bifolio and one quire choose the same 'free' words (z 7-10, both transcriptions); the generators show nothing. The residual sorts by physical writing unit, not by section or illustration type |
| v45:V-45.1.4 | POSITIVE CONTROL for 1.3: planted Latin, pages sharing an Isidore chapter (within book), also with adjacent pages excluded | LAw raw chapter z +5.1 (non-adjacent +5.5), residual +3.2 (+2.2); LAl raw +1.5 (+2.3), residual +1.1 (+1.0) | A word-level code leaves its topic in the residual (z 2-3); a letter-level code is nearly invisible at page level (topic signal is in letter n-grams, not letter shares). So page-level residual tracking can detect word-level content, not letter-level |
| v45:V-45.1.V | Cycle verdict | - | The residual is NOT structureless: it repeats within bifolios and quires (z 7-10, ZL and IT2a), where rule-only and misfit generators give 0. But the same unit-level coherence is v8/v41 (bifolio = one moment in time): drift or content? Cycle 2 asks whether the residual tracks the drawings once bifolio, quire, leaf and adjacency are partialled. Grade B (measurement): residual bifolio/quire coherence; Grade C: that it is content |
| v45:V-45.2.1 | Partial Mantel, 119 Voynich herbal pages: drawing similarity (pre-registered v38 'fresh' composite, EfficientNet-B0 + DINOv2) vs text similarity, residualised on language, hand, quire, bifolio, leaf, adjacency, log page distance, text length, drawing area; pages permuted within language x hand (2000x). Text similarity from raw tf-idf, from residual usage (observed - rule-expected), from residual on shared words only, and from the rule-expected usage itself | V ZL raw z +1.66 (p 0.056), residual +0.97 (p 0.16), residual-shared +1.06, expected -0.90. IT2a raw +1.80 (p 0.045), residual +1.72 (p 0.047), shared +1.77, expected -0.74 | Faint v38 link reproduced on the surface; removing what the rules predict does NOT strengthen it (ZL weaker, IT2a equal) |
| v45:V-45.2.2 | NEGATIVES: generator output on the same 119 page skeletons, same drawings | GEN0 raw -2.06, residual -1.30; GEN1 raw +1.00, residual -0.07; PL raw -1.43, residual -0.98 | Null band of the test roughly -2 to +1 |
| v45:V-45.2.3 | POSITIVE: Gerard Herball (1636) text pushed through planted verbose word codes, same Voynich rules, against its woodcuts (v38 confounds: adjacency, distance, length, woodcut area) | Gerard plain text z +6.2. Code with 400 buckets (GEw): raw -0.38, residual +0.64. Letter code (GEl): raw -2.39, residual -0.06. Near-nomenclator code, 2000 buckets (GEw2000): raw +1.32 (p 0.10), residual +2.38 (p 0.014), residual-shared +2.47 (p 0.010) | Calibration: homophonic word codes destroy most of a real herbal's picture link (6.2 -> ~0-2.5); where any survives, the residual carries it better than the surface (+1.1 z). That 'residual > surface' signature is what a hidden message predicts |
| v45:V-45.2.V | Cycle verdict | Signature test: planted residual - raw = +1.06 (GEw2000), +1.02 (GEw), +2.33 (GEl); Voynich -0.69 (ZL), -0.08 (IT2a); generators +0.76, -1.07, +0.45 | The Voynich does not show the planted signature: its picture link, if any, sits in the surface vocabulary the rules already predict, not in the free choices. Grade B (negative, low power: the positive control itself reaches only z 2.4, so a weak message under a homophonic code would be missed) |
| v45:V-45.3.1 | Three streams per corpus in reading order: SURF (every word), SURP (only words in the top 30% of surprise minus model entropy: the choices the rules least expected), PIT (which alternative was chosen: probability-integral transform among the allowed words, 8 bins + 1 for words outside the allowed set). Battery on the first 6,000 tokens: Zipf slope, TTR, hapax share, burstiness (repeat within 100 stream tokens / same after shuffling within section), lag-1/2/4 MI minus within-page shuffle, arrow (held-out bigram forward minus backward), held-out bigram gain over unigram | SURP stream: V zipf -0.28, TTR 0.71, hapax 0.77, burst 1.96, MI excess lag 1/2/4 -0.019/-0.025/-0.027; IT2a -0.29, 0.72, 0.78, 1.82, -0.019/-0.020/-0.015. GEN0/GEN1 -0.37/-0.36, 0.45, 0.54, 1.23/1.12, about -0.01 to +0.004. PL (copy-and-vary misfit) -0.27, 0.49, 0.51, 2.68, +0.021/-0.001/-0.005. Planted: LAw -0.19, 0.34, 0.24, 1.41, ~0; LAl -0.24, 0.25, 0.25, 1.07, ~0; GEw2000 (near-nomenclator) -0.17, 0.46, 0.36, 1.53, +0.056/+0.025/+0.021 | Only the near-nomenclator plant makes a residual stream with language-like sequential dependence (MI excess +0.02 to +0.06 at lags 1-4); the bucket codes do not (homophones dilute word identity). The Voynich surprise stream is the opposite of language: lexically richer than any control (TTR 0.71, hapax 0.77), bursty like the copy-and-vary generator (1.8-2.0 vs PL 2.7, GEN 1.1-1.2), and with NEGATIVE MI excess at lags 1-4 (neighbouring surprising choices share less than random same-page pairs) |
| v45:V-45.3.2 | SURF reference (full surface, same battery) | V MI excess lag 1/2/4 -0.003/-0.055/-0.097, IT2a -0.019/-0.062/-0.096; GEN0 +0.071/-0.023/-0.035; PL +0.018/-0.026/-0.026; LAw +0.042/+0.013/+0.002; LAl +0.102/+0.017/-0.012; GEw2000 +0.102/+0.069/+0.032 | The Voynich surface also has the most negative short-range MI excess of all corpora (lag 4: -0.10 vs -0.03 generators, >= 0 for every encoded text): nearby words avoid sharing information more than page composition implies (anti-repetition / local dissimilation). Grade C (plug-in MI; needs a debiased estimator) |
| v45:V-45.3.3 | PIT stream (which alternative) | Lag-1 MI excess: V +0.0064, IT2a +0.0069; GEN0 +0.0031, GEN1 +0.0014, PL +0.0018; LAw +0.0015, LAl +0.0008, GEw2000 +0.0080. Held-out order-2 gain: V +0.010, IT2a +0.013; generators +0.002 to +0.004; GEw2000 +0.007 | The Voynich 'which alternative' stream has 2-4x the sequential dependence of its generators, at the level of the planted nomenclator code, in both transcriptions. | v49:V-49.F | THE WORDS THAT BREAK THE RULES ARE THE CONTENT (3 cycles; tools/v49_*.py; loops/v49_cycle1-3.txt, v49_final.txt): off-table words (v45 rule model) split into opening-line and body lexicons and tested as subject terms / borrowed names. Controls: a real Italian herbal with Latin binomials (Brumati 1844) through a planted rule-bound glyph code, planted subject terms, generator + planted rare-word noise. Tests: own-page recurrence, illustration-tied reuse, position, glyph structure, refit with gallows merged, n-gram separability, lexicon typology map, 4,000 random spelling views with held-out pages | Latin names come out off-table (86-87% vs 33%). Voynich opening off-rule words: own-page recurrence -0.010 (planted +0.06 to +0.10); flat along the line; stranger by +0.21 bits/glyph, but +0.02 after the gallows merge (Latin layer +0.17); separability with gallows and ch/sh removed 0.56 = noise 0.55 (Latin 0.66); typology distance at the split-half floor; random views held-out +0.24 (real herbal +2.1) | Grade B (negative): no subject-term behaviour, no second language layer; Grade A: the opening line is a spelling regime (gallows) over body vocabulary. v45 Grade C demoted. Not cracked |
| v49:V-49.1.0 | LEXICON. Off-table tokens = words outside every choice the v45 rule model (v26 minimal genome + backoff, 2-fold page cross-fit) allows at their slot. Split OFFpf (paragraph-first line) / OFFb (body). Corpora through the identical crossfit: V (ZL3b), VI (IT2a), generators GEN0/GEN1/PL; NEW negative GENN = GEN0 + planted rare-word noise (novel spliced words, 20% of opening-line tokens, 6% of body); NEW positive GENT = GEN0 + planted subject terms (novel term early in each paragraph's first line, repeated 1-2x in the paragraph, 40% shared with another page of the same section); NEW real-herbal positives BRe = Brumati, Flora medico-economica (1844; Italian with a Latin binomial in each species entry; 405 entries, 41k words, entry = paragraph, 9 words/line) pushed through a planted rule-bound glyph code (letter -> glyph chunk, t->k after o, ch->sh 60% in opening lines and line-initial words, optional q-), BRf = same with the Latin binomial moved to the head of its entry (medieval-herbal order). Ground truth: binomial tokens | Off-table share opening/body: V 0.42/0.27, VI 0.42/0.27; GEN0 0.20/0.17, PL 0.26/0.25; GENN 0.35/0.22; GENT 0.25/0.19; BRe 0.39/0.33; BRf 0.45/0.32. In the herbals 86-87% of Latin-name tokens are off-table vs 33% of Italian tokens; in BRf Latin is 23% of OFFpf (1% of all text) | Planted code passes the basic split: borrowed names come out as the off-rule lexicon (Grade A, control) |

Grade C guess: the edges are linkage fields chaining adjacent words (would support: the edge slot predicts the neighbour's identity beyond its edge glyph; would kill: the effect vanishes when the r/s + a- and y + q- pairs are treated as misplaced spaces) |

| v51:V-51.F | LET A SPEECH MODEL LISTEN (3 cycles; loops/v51_cycle1-3.txt). Controls: Latin, German, Czech, Italian, consonantal Hebrew, Esperanto rendered with the same mappings; nulls: unit shuffle and Markov-1 resynthesis of every text; generators on Voynich units: self-citation, table grille, Markov-2, uniform gibberish | Languages beat their own shuffles weakly and unstably (z -0.3 to +3.7; different languages pass on different mapping sets); Markov-1 resyntheses beat shuffles about as much; structureless generators fail (gibberish -0.2, grille -0.8) but Voynich-fitted generators pass (+1.5, +1.6). Voynich ZL +1.3 to +2.0, IT2a +2.8. Mapping search: best mappings generalise to held-out text halves for Latin and Markov-2 only (p 0.045, 0.023), not Voynich (p 0.52); vowel recovery fails for German and Esperanto. Sections: stars > bio > herbal > pharma nominally (stars - pharma z +2.0) but split-half rank reliability rho +0.18. Heard-language shift: own language ranks 14-92 of 99 for the controls | Not cracked. Grade B (negative, instrument-limited): through random phone mappings, a speech listener hears low-order (bigram) sequential structure and nothing that separates language from Voynich-fitted generators; the Voynich sits inside both bands. No section reliably sounds more speech-like. Grade C demoted: 'Voynich = generator profile (language-ID up, token confidence down)' failed its pre-registered replication |
| v51:V-51.1.1 | 160 random mappings x 28 corpora (4,480 clips). Positive control: each language vs its own unit shuffle, same mapping | Composite paired z: Esperanto +3.7, Czech +2.5, Hebrew +2.5, Italian +0.8, German +0.4, Latin +0.4 (differences 0.02-0.18 sd) | The listener hears language order over shuffled units, but weakly: 3 of 6 languages pass z > 2. Low-power instrument |
| v51:V-51.1.2 | Kill control: does the listener separate language from simple sequence models? Language vs its Markov-1 resynthesis; Markov-1 vs shuffle | Language vs mk1: Hebrew +2.0, others -1.5 to +1.4. mk1 vs shuffle: Esperanto +2.2, Latin +1.7, Czech +1.2 | Most of what it hears is bigram structure, which a Markov chain has. It does not hear 'language' beyond bigrams |
| v51:V-51.1.3 | Generators vs the Voynich unit shuffle (gibberish must fail) | Uniform gibberish -0.2, table grille -0.8 (both fail, as required); order-2 Markov +1.6; self-citation +1.5 | The generators that copy Voynich statistics sound as speech-like as the languages; only structureless generators fail |

Dropped |
| v51:V-51.1.6 | Score profile (post hoc): which score carries the gain over shuffle | Languages gain on lex (token confidence): Czech +2.7, Esperanto +2.5, Hebrew +0.8, German +0.7, Latin +0.2, Italian -1.4. Would support: replicates on fresh mappings (cycle 2). Would kill: lex gain for Voynich >= 0 on fresh mappings, or languages' lex gain not positive |

Held-out gain of top-15 vs random-15 discovery mappings (Mann-Whitney, one-sided) and rank correlation of discovery vs held-out gain over the 30 | Latin +0.98 vs -0.16 (p 0.045, rho +0.14); German +0.97 vs +0.59 (p 0.25); Czech +0.01 vs +0.54 (p 0.65); Esperanto -0.32 vs -0.24 (p 0.55); Hebrew -0.59 vs -0.09 (p 0.73); Markov-2 generator +2.50 vs +0.37 (p 0.023, rho +0.35); self-citation +0.71 vs -0.14 (p 0.25); Voynich +0.14 vs +0.03 (p 0.52, rho -0.10) | 2 of 8 corpora at p < 0.05, one of them a generator; 4 of 5 languages fail. | v51:V-51.2.3 | Positive control for using the listener as a vowel detector: in the top mappings, are the TRUE vowels of Latin, German, Esperanto (rank < 20) assigned the vowel class more often than in all mappings? | Vowel-class lift on true vowels vs consonants: Latin +0.066 vs +0.019; German -0.077 vs -0.027; Esperanto -0.012 vs +0.036 | Fails on 2 of 3 alphabets with known vowels. The listener cannot recover which letters are vowels, so its choice for the Voynich (benched gallows cth/ckh 'vowel-like'; o, l, r, n 'consonant-like') means nothing. Not reported as a reading |
| v51:V-51.2.V | Cycle verdict | - | Massive random mapping search through a speech listener: no mapping generalises for the Voynich (p 0.52), and the controls show why: the instrument cannot find generalising mappings or recover vowels even for Latin, German or Esperanto. Grade B (negative, instrument-limited): rendered-speech listening carries no usable information about Voynich glyph classes |
| v51:V-51.3.1 | Positive control, fresh mappings: language vs own shuffle (composite paired z) | Esperanto +2.4, Czech +1.7, Hebrew +1.2, Latin +0.7, Italian -0.2, German -0.3 (cycle 1 on other mappings: +3.7, +2.5, +2.5, +0.4, +0.8, +0.4) | Weak and unstable: 1 of 6 languages passes z > 2 here, and the per-language ranking changes between mapping sets |
| v51:V-51.3.2 | Voynich sections and hands vs their own shuffles | Stars/recipes +2.6 (win 0.63), biological +2.0, herbal +1.3, pharma -0.3; hand 1 +2.7, hand 2 +1.2, hand 3 +0.7; whole ZL +1.3. Paired section contrasts: stars - pharma z +2.0, biological - pharma +1.6, stars - herbal +1.0, hand 1 - hand 3 +1.3 | Nominal order stars > bio > herbal > pharma. Not significant after the 4 contrasts, and the same size as the spread between real languages (Esperanto - German z +2.0) |
| v51:V-51.3.3 | Is the speech-gain ranking reliable? Split-half (odd vs even mappings) Spearman rank correlation of gains over the 14 corpora | rho +0.18 (p 0.53) | Not reliable: the ranking of sections (and of languages) does not reproduce across mapping halves. V-51.3.2 order is noise. Grade B (negative) |
| v51:V-51.3.4 | Heard-language shift (log P(language) in order minus shuffled, 99 languages). Positive control: does each control text shift the listener toward its OWN language? | Own-language rank of 99: Hebrew 14, German 33, Latin 38, Czech 43, Italian 92 (chance mean 50); own z -1.1 to +1.0 | Control fails: under random phone mappings the listener's language guess carries no trace of the source language. The Voynich shift (ZL up: Urdu, Malayalam, Hindi) is not readable |
| v51:V-51.3.5 | Split-half reliability of each shift profile (odd vs even mappings, Pearson over 99 languages) | Voynich ZL +0.02, sections -0.03 to +0.44, hands +0.17 to +0.58; languages +0.09 to +0.73 (Esperanto) | The Voynich language shift is noise (r 0.02). Dropped |
| v51:V-51.3.V | Cycle verdict | - | No section or hand of the Voynich sounds reliably more speech-like than another, and the listener's language guess is uninformative even for known languages. Grade B (negative) |
| v54:V-54.1.1 | Near-repeat families: pairs of 3-word passages (total <= 3 edits) in real text vs 5 nulls | ZL 22,683 pairs (208 exact); posshuf 9,478, pageshuf 8,735, lineshuf 6,981, markov 10,509, selfcit 829. IT2a 20,044 vs 6,577-8,585. Ratio real/best null 2.2-2.4 (Voynich); planted herbal 51,663 vs 1,764-6,108 (x8-29); German 44,391 vs 9,190-12,397 (x3.6) | Grade A measurement: the Voynich has more near-repeated 3-word passages than chance, but far less excess than either control text |
| v54:V-54.1.2 | Distance spectrum (pairs with 0/1/2/3 edits; nulls = mean of 3 seeds) | ZL 208/1,781/7,252/13,442 vs posshuf 31/496/2,746/6,186: excess x6.7/3.6/2.6/2.2. IT2a x4.9/3.7/2.5/2.1. German x92/9.2/2.3/1.0. Planted herbal x2000/170/28/7. In real texts the excess sits in the close pairs (German: 86% of excess pairs at <= 1 edit); Voynich: only 11% of its excess is at <= 1 edit, most is at 2-3 edits | Grade B: Voynich near-repeats are a broad similarity field (neighbouring words resemble neighbouring words), not copies of fixed passages with a few spelling changes. Witness-like close pairs exist but are a thin layer (about 1,460 excess pairs) |
| v54:V-54.1.3 | Collation profile: edits per pair and where in the word they fall (initial/medial/final variation rate per unit occurrence) | Edits per pair: ZL 2.50, IT 2.50 vs nulls 2.58-2.61 (almost chance); planted herbal 1.52 vs 2.57-2.75; German 0.97 vs 2.25-2.52; clean herbal 0.11. Voynich variation is word-initial heavy (I 0.19, M 0.09, F 0.05) exactly as in its nulls (I 0.20-0.23, M 0.09, F 0.05-0.06); German real repeats vary less at both edges than chance (I 0.07 F 0.06 vs 0.13/0.21) | Grade A: the differences between Voynich witnesses are as large and placed as the differences between chance-paired passages. A real text's repeats differ less, and differently, from chance pairs |
| v54:V-54.1.4 | Variant classes, top collation ops (normalised count) | Planted herbal: the top 7 ops are exactly the planted classes (h deleted; i~j, u~v, i~y, j~y, m~n; doubled consonants), PASS. Its nulls give h, u~v weakly plus chance vowel swaps (e~a). German: diacritics and superscripts dropped or added, j, r~ß(=long-s family): real scribal classes. Voynich (ZL and IT2a agree): rare gallows and p/f deleted (x, f, p, F, K, T, s, P), k~t, ch~sh, l~r, d and q deleted. The SAME top list comes out of every Voynich null (posshuf, pageshuf, markov) in nearly the same order | Grade B: the Voynich "variant classes" (gallows/p/f optional, k~t, ch~sh, l~r, q- and d optional) are what any near-neighbour pairing of Voynich words produces; collation adds nothing beyond lexical neighbourhood. The method itself works (planted classes recovered at rank 1-7) |
| v54:V-54.1.5 | Payload vs padding, per-unit variation rate (share of occurrences involved in a collation op) | Most variable: P 0.97, f 0.93, p 0.84, T 0.76, K 0.74, m 0.73, s 0.66, t 0.50, S 0.33, r 0.29, C 0.28, d 0.25, l 0.22, k 0.18, q 0.14, e 0.13; least: o, a, i, n, y (<= 0.10). Nulls: same order within +-0.05 | Grade B measurement: "padding" = gallows, p/f, m, s, word-initial slot; "payload" = the o/a/i/n/y/e vowel-and-coda frame. Grade C: because the nulls give the same profile, this is a property of the lexicon, not evidence of meaning-preserving copying |
| v54:V-54.1.6 | Cycle verdict | Method passes its planted control and sees real German scribal variation. The Voynich has an excess of near-repeated passages (x2.2-2.4, held in both transcriptions), but its witnesses differ like chance pairs | Not cracked. Next: does a reduced alphabet built from the collation carry more page information on held-out pages than random reductions (cycle 2)? |
| v54:V-54.2.1 | Planted control (Brumati herbal with planted medieval spelling, opaque symbols) | Recovered ops K=4: i~j, h deleted, i~y, u~v (K=8 adds j~y, n/nn, m~n, r/rr) = all planted, no false class. Witness pairs made identical 76% (random 18%). Held out: section accuracy +5.2 points (z 14), bigram gain +0.78 bits/token (z 25), scribe accuracy -9 points (z -16: the spelling layer, which is what carries the scribe, is gone) | PASS. A real text's collation classes are spelling: removing them raises content information and erases the copyist |
| v54:V-54.2.2 | Clean herbal and real 14th-15th c. German | Clean herbal: ops v, x, j~v, t~s (inflection noise), section z 0.9, bigram z 3.8. German: j, i/ii, r~long-s family, x; section and position at ceiling (z 0.6 / -0.4), bigram +0.03-0.11 bits (z 4-6) | Real text with mild variation: gain mostly in word-pair predictability |
| v54:V-54.2.3 | Generators | Markov (unit trigram per section): ops l~r, a~o, ch deleted; all scores BELOW random (z -1.5 to -3.6). Planted herbal Markov: below random (bigram z -8). Self-citation (copy-and-modify per section): ops delete a, d, e, i; ABOVE random on every variable (z 2.5-4.4) and bigram (z 2.4-3.3) | Markov generator correctly fails. The copy-and-modify generator passes: the gain test alone cannot separate meaning from self-citation |
| v54:V-54.2.4 | Voynich ZL3b and IT2a (agree) | Ops over-represented in real witnesses (K=4): e/ee collapse, i/ii collapse, q- deleted, ch~sh (IT) or ch~cth (ZL); K=8 adds k~t, l~r, ch~k, r~s. Witness pairs made identical 4-5% (random 1.2-1.4%). Held out K=4: Currier language +0.8 to +1.3 points (z 3.0-3.2), hand +0.9/+2.2 points (z 1.6/4.5), section +0.5/+1.4 (z 1.1/2.6), quire z 1.8/2.4, bigram +0.046/+0.050 bits/token (z 7.5/7.2). K=8 weaker except bigram (z 4.0/6.6) | Grade B measurement: collapsing e/ee, i/ii and q- makes Voynich witnesses agree and helps held-out prediction above random reductions, as in real texts and as in the self-citation generator; size is 1/5 to 1/15 of the planted scribal layer |
| v54:V-54.2.5 | Discriminator: what happens to the WRITER variable | Planted scribes: hand information falls (z -16) when the collation classes are merged. Voynich: hand information RISES (z +1.6 / +4.5), as in the self-citation generator (z +3.2 to +3.7) | Grade B (negative for the spelling reading): the Voynich variant classes are not scribe-specific spelling habits; merging them behaves like removing generator noise. Grade C reading: e-run length, i-run length and q- are free "padding" that hides a smaller set of word skeletons. Would support: skeletons recur across sections more than random skeletons do in held-out text. Would kill: the e/ee, i/ii, q- choices predicted by a neighbouring word or by line position (then they are grammar or layout, v40/v52) |
| v54:V-54.2.6 | Cycle verdict | The textual-critic's reduced alphabet works on real copying and fails on a Markov generator, but a copy-and-modify generator passes it too. Voynich gain is real but small and generator-like | Not cracked. Next: stemma test (do variants cluster by hand or quire?) and transcriber disagreements as a third witness (cycle 3) |
| v54:V-54.3.1 | Stemma, planted control: three scribes with different spelling preferences in 6-page blocks | Scribe MI 8,880 vs 1,286 (z 14.8, p 0.005) | PASS: the test finds copyists from their variants |
| v54:V-54.3.2 | Stemma, generator controls | Unit-Markov trained per section: hand z -1.5, quire z 0.2 (no false stemma). Unit-Markov trained per HAND: hand z 10.2 (a hand-specific generator is found). Section (unstratified) z 14-18 in real and Markov alike (section statistics alone produce it) | Calibrated: hand-specific glyph habits would be seen |
| v54:V-54.3.3 | Stemma, Voynich | ZL: hand z 1.2 (p 0.09), quire z 2.5 (p 0.005), Currier language z 1.6 (p 0.04). IT2a: hand z 2.5 (p 0.01), quire z 1.3 (p 0.09). The two transcriptions disagree on which is significant | Grade B negative: inside a section, the choice between witness variants carries at most a trace of hand or quire (z 1-2.5, vs 14.8 for planted scribes and 10.2 for a hand-specific generator). The variants are not copyists' spellings that cluster like a stemma |
| v54:V-54.3.4 | Transcriber disagreements as witnesses | Transcriber classes: c deleted, g~m, r~s, a~o, F~P, f~p, gallows~h. Rank correlation transcriber vs scribal-witness classes 0.43, transcriber vs chance-pair classes 0.45, scribal vs chance 0.89; transcriber vs (scribal excess over chance) 0.19. Ops most over-represented in real witnesses: F deleted (19 vs 15 in a smaller null; tiny), ch~F, r~s (572 real vs 89 chance-pair edits; x2.2 per unit occurrence; also the 3rd transcriber confusion), i/ii (x1.5), a deleted, K~T, n~r, ch~cth | Grade B: scribal witness classes are mostly chance neighbour classes (0.89). Grade C: r~s and i/ii, which the transcribers also confuse, are over-represented between witnesses; part of the "variation" may be reading ambiguity of the glyphs, not the scribe's choice. Would kill: r~s witness pairs on clean, unambiguous images |
| v54:V-54.3.5 | Cycle verdict | No stemma; variant classes = neighbour classes plus a reading-ambiguity residue | Not cracked |
| v54:V-54.4.1 | Near-repeat spectrum vs word-n-gram resynthesis (per section, line lengths kept) | A word-bigram chain gives the Voynich 3,558 exact 3-word repeats against 208 real (x17); real texts get about their own number (German 17,182 vs 18,705; clean herbal 60,775 vs 66,554). Unigram resynthesis gives 22 | Not a usable null: on the Voynich the chain loops through its most frequent pairs (qokeedy-type runs). Replaced by the exact permutation test V-54.4.2. Logged so it is not rerun |
| v54:V-54.4.2 | Passage repetition index PRI: pairs of occurrences of a word pair (a,b) in one section that are also followed by the same word c, observed / expected when the successors of b are shuffled among b's occurrences (200x) | ZL 110 vs 79.5 (PRI 1.39, z 3.4); IT2a 1.33 (z 2.7); Currier B 1.48 (z 3.4); Currier A 0.96 (z -0.2). Controls: bigram chain 1.00 (z 0.5, sanity); unit-Markov 1.31 (z 1.6, small-number noise); self-citation 1.17 (z 0.6). Real: clean herbal 1.25 (z 74), planted herbal 1.39 (z 26), German 2.33 (z 45), CATMuS Latin 1.64 (z 38), CATMuS Italian 1.64 (z 6), Latin 2.38 (z 40), Hebrew 1.70 (z 22) | Grade B measurement: Voynich 3-word passages recur slightly beyond word-pair chance (z ~3, Currier B only), with a ratio inside the real-text range but on very few cases (about 30 excess pairs in 35,000 words, against hundreds to thousands in real texts of the same size) |
| v54:V-54.4.3 | Third word as witness: when (a,b) recurs, is the next word a ONE-EDIT VARIANT of last time's next word more often than under successor shuffle? | Real texts with spelling or inflection variation: German 1.60 (z 17), CATMuS Latin 1.37 (z 9.5), Latin 1.58 (z 5), planted herbal 1.25 (z 14). Printed herbal without variation: 1.02 (z 0.4). Voynich: ZL 272 vs 269 (1.01, z 0.2), IT2a 1.05 (z 0.7). Generators: self-citation 1.09 (z 0.5), Markov 0.89 | Grade C (underpowered): the Voynich shows no excess of variant third words. But its exact excess is so small (~30 pairs) that variant excess / exact excess is 0.11 +- 0.55 (ZL) and 0.52 +- 0.75 (IT2a), compatible with real texts (0.08-0.46). Would support the spelling-variant reading: a larger transliteration-independent corpus (all ~5,400 lines incl. labels and circular text) giving variant excess z > 3. Would kill: variant ratio staying at 1.0 when the exact excess is doubled |
| v54:V-54.4.4 | Cycle verdict | Passages exist only as a thin layer (Currier B), and the test that should see their spelling variants lacks power | Not cracked |
| v54:V-54.5.1 | Exact and variant targets, pooled directions | Voynich paragraph text: exact 330 vs 206 (x1.61, z 7.3) ZL, 291 vs 192 (z 6.3) IT2a; variant 873 vs 768 (x1.14, z 4.0) ZL, 825 vs 714 (z 4.1) IT2a. All line types (labels, rings): exact x2.0/1.9 (z 16/13), variant x1.07 (z 2.1/1.7). Controls: German exact z 86 / variant z 28; CATMuS Latin z 76 / 23; CATMuS Italian z 13 / 2.6; Latin z 72 / 10; planted herbal z 54 / 28; printed herbal z 136 / 0.6 (no variation, correctly none). Generators: self-citation z 1.3 / 1.8; unit-Markov 2.6 / -1.4 | Grade B: with enough power, recurring Voynich contexts DO bring back one-edit variants beyond chance, in both transcriptions. Passages are re-written with variation, as in copied real texts |
| v54:V-54.5.2 | How much variation per repeat (variant excess / exact excess) | Voynich ZL 0.85 +- 0.21, IT2a 1.12 +- 0.27 (all lines 0.24-0.26, labels repeat exactly). Real texts: German 0.13, CATMuS Latin 0.13, CATMuS Italian 0.15, Latin 0.07, printed herbal 0.002; heavy planted medieval spelling 0.53 +- 0.02. Self-citation generator 1.8 +- 1.0 | Grade B: in running text a Voynich passage that comes back is about as likely to come back changed by one glyph as unchanged, 6-8x the rate of real medieval copying and above even the heavily planted spelling control. Only the copy-and-modify generator sits as high (with low power). Grade C reading: the Voynich writer did not copy with free spelling; he re-generated each recurrence with a mutation (or the varied glyph carries something that changes between mentions). Would kill: a real medieval text (e.g. a heavily abbreviated recipe book) reaching 0.8 |
| v54:V-54.5.3 | The critic's variant classes: ops in context-anchored variants minus the same under target shuffles (20x) | Planted control: i~j, u~v, h, m~n, i~y, j~y, doubling: all planted classes, ranks 1-11, PASS. German: i~y, j, v, i, i~v, i/ii, e, acute/diaeresis/underline marks, n~nasal stroke: genuine medieval spelling classes, PASS. Voynich (ZL / IT2a agree): ch~sh (+20/+18), d~e (+15/+17), d deleted (+14/+17), e/ee (+13/+20), q- deleted (+13/+11), cth~d (+6/+10), ch~k, r~s (ZL). Excess sits word-initially (+30/+33) and medially (+61/+77), little at the end (+21/+4) | Grade B measurement: the context-anchored Voynich variant classes are ch~sh, e~ee~d (the -edy/-eey/-ey family), and optional q-. Grade C: these are the "padding" the writer is free to change between mentions; the rest of the word (o/a/i/n/y/l/r frame and gallows identity) is the payload kept across mentions. Would kill: the same classes coming out of a junction or line-position model (v40, v52) with no recurring context |
| v54:V-54.5.4 | Cycle verdict | The textual-critic machine works on real copying (German, Latin, planted). On the Voynich it finds witnesses and variant classes, but the variation rate per repeat is copy-and-modify-generator-like, not scribe-like | Not cracked |

| v54:V-54.F.2 | Variant classes from all near-repeats | Gallows and p/f optional, k~t, ch~sh, l~r, d and q optional; the same list comes out of every null | Grade B: lexical neighbourhood, not copying |
| v54:V-54.F.3 | Payload / padding | Most variable: gallows, p/f, m, s, word-initial slot (I 0.19 vs F 0.05); stable: o, a, i, n, y, e frame. Same in nulls | Grade B measurement, C as meaning |
| v54:V-54.F.4 | Reduced alphabet (e/ee, i/ii, q-, ch~sh/cth) on held-out pages | Beats 30 random reductions: Currier language z 3, bigram +0.05 bits/token z 7, section z 1-2.6; but the self-citation generator passes too (z 2.4-4.4), and writer information RISES (z +1.6/+4.5) where scribal spelling falls (z -16) | Grade B: not scribal spelling; the gain test cannot separate meaning from copy-and-modify |
| v54:V-54.F.5 | Stemma | Variant choice inside a section tracks hand or quire only weakly (z 1.2-2.5, transcriptions disagree) | Grade B negative |

Variants per exact repeat 0.85-1.12, vs real medieval copying 0.07-0.15 and heavy planted spelling 0.53; only the copy-and-modify generator is as high (1.8 +- 1.0) | Grade B: repeats are regenerated with a mutation, not copied with spelling |
| v54:V-54.F.9 | Critic's classes (context-anchored) | ch~sh, e~ee~d (-edy/-eey/-ey), q- optional, cth~d; word-initial and medial | Grade B measurement; Grade C: the padding the writer varies between mentions |

## Summary

See FINDINGS.md for the state of knowledge before 4 Oct 2026.
